`pnpm test` includes a tracked-file guard that fails when the removed auth provider is reintroduced.

## Shipping

Follow the Nexo flow in `AGENTS.md`.
Keep changes atomic, verify locally, capture the run under `nexo/`, commit with a Conventional Commit message, and push `master` after Gate 2 passes.

<!-- nexo:managed:start version=4 sha256=02fd055249adb37ac71ec0db87e5e313c575eadc762d972ac81c07a1a398a84b -->
## Nexo workflow contract

Nexo owns the delivery workflow while a Nexo flow is active.
Work moves through Frame, Plan, Execute, Verify, and Capture.
Feature and batch flows plan the complete initial slice set before execution, then adapt only within the finite runtime policy.

The human owns WHAT and why.
The agent owns HOW.
Gate 1 is human approval of WHAT and is skipped only by explicit autopilot.
Gate 2 is local verification and is never skipped.
Gate 3 is the human-approved release cut and is never automatic.

Verification is tiered.
Each slice runs its named locked oracle tests plus lint on changed files.
Each integrated wave runs the full suite, full lint, and security checks once.
Each feature runs mutation testing once after all waves are green.
Execute and Verify use separate agents whenever the host supports them and the user has not explicitly required single-agent execution.

Delivery is local trunk flow.
Verified short-lived branches merge serially to `main` with no pull request and no hosted CI requirement.
Promotion to `staging` and `production` exists only when `nexo/state.json` opts into it, and every promotion is fast-forward-only.
The user never commits by hand because Nexo owns branch, commit, verification, merge, and cleanup.

Autopilot never waits for a human and never expands a budget.
A blocker or exhausted budget is recorded in `AUDIT.md`, unfinished work is parked, owned worktrees and processes are cleaned up, and the run returns a partial completion report.
<!-- nexo:managed:end -->
# Prompt — FXL Vendas: CRM unificado (v1)

> Rodar no FXL Harness (Claude Code), na pasta do repo
> `/Users/cauetpinciara/Documents/fxl/projects/06--PRODUCT--fxl-sales`, com
> `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=~/.claude-empresa`. Base de contexto: `.nexo/tmp/vendas-modos-analise.md`.

---

/nexo-feature --autopilot

> Modo autopilot (padrão Nexo): sem Gate 1 humano dentro da run; bloqueios viram `AUDIT.md` + pendência e a run segue. Gate 2 (Verify separado) nunca é pulado.

## 0. Regras invioláveis desta run (ler antes de tudo)

1. **Nada de produção.** Sem SSH, túnel, banco real, dados reais de cliente, `make update-app`, `git push`, deploy, Coolify, Vercel, Hub de produção. Trunk local é **`master`** (ver `nexo/state.json` → `delivery.trunk`); o merge acontece só no `master` LOCAL. O repo está em `delivery.mode: "promotion"`, mas **esta run nunca chama `/nexo-ship` nem `/nexo-ship-prod-ready`** — produção só depois, via `/nexo-ship`, com OK explícito do Cauê em outra sessão.
2. **Worktrees** em `<repo>/.worktrees/<run-id>/...` via `nexo-worktree.sh` (flows/feature.md). **Nunca apagar branches nem worktrees** — nem os desta run, nem os existentes. Onde o fluxo Nexo mandaria limpar (cleanup do `#### 9 · Report`, "delete branch"), **pular** e registrar no relatório como "mantido por ordem do Cauê".
3. **Modelos explícitos por papel, nunca `inherit`:**
   | Papel | Modelo | Faz |
   |---|---|---|
   | Orquestrador (host) | **Opus 5.5** | Frame, plano abstrato de fases, gates, merge. **Não implementa código.** |
   | Planner / plan-checker / Verify (Gate 2) / scribe | **`sonnet`** (versão atual) | detalha cada fase em slices; Verify separado de quem implementou |
   | Executor | **`haiku`** (versão atual) | implementa Red→Green→Refactor |
   | Reviewer (code review por wave) | **Codex CLI `-m gpt-6-astra high`** (explícito; o padrão global `gpt-6.1-sol medium` NÃO vale aqui) | papel `reviewer` do Nexo (`agents/reviewer.md`), via `python3 "$NEXO_SCRIPTS/nexo-agent.py" run reviewer --repo <worktree> --run "$RUN_ID" --slice <slice>` (B2 em `flows/feature.md`); roda pela **assinatura do Codex (login ChatGPT), nunca por API key** |
   Todo spawn passa `--model` / `model:` explícito e grava `--agent-model` no status (engine.md `## Model tiers`, "The host model never reaches a leg"). Todo comando `claude` roda com `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=~/.claude-empresa`. Sem API paga direta.
4. **Testes de migração só com dados isolados**: Postgres local/efêmero do `docker-compose.yml`/test harness (`apps/api/test/`), fixtures sintéticas. O guard `scripts/__tests__/local-database-guard.test.mjs` deve continuar passando.
5. **Ids técnicos não mudam**: `fxlAppId`/`name` `fxl-sales` (package.json raiz, `packages/auth-fake/src/index.ts:99`), pacotes `@fxl-sales/*`, domínios `sales.fxlbusiness.com` / `sales-api.fxlbusiness.com` (`apps/api/src/middleware/app-auth.ts:~434`), envs `SALES_*`, eventos `fxl-sales.obligation.upserted|settlement.recorded|settlement.reversed` (`apps/api/src/domains/integration/events.ts`), chaves de localStorage `fxl-sales.*` (`apps/web/src/auth/session-recovery.ts`, `apps/web/src/dev/*`). Tabelas `sales_ops_*` e identificadores `salesOps*` também ficam.
6. **Fases pequenas**, cada uma com Verify (Gate 2) e testes. Se algo exigir produção ou dado real, **não fazer**: registrar como pendência.
7. O relatório final **confirma o modelo efetivamente usado em cada papel** (lido dos result files / status, não presumido).

## 1. Contexto (o que existe hoje — confirme relendo o código)

- Um app, duas "edições": `SalesEdition = 'full' | 'leads'` em `packages/shared-utils/src/sales-edition.ts`; derivada por request do token do Hub (`entitlements.modules` contém `sales.edition.leads`) em `apps/api/src/middleware/app-auth.ts:276`; gate de capability em `apps/api/src/middleware/require-capability.ts`. Edição `leads` = Construbom (só `clients` + `import`).
- Funil: tabela `sales_ops_lead_stages` (migr. `apps/api/drizzle/0022_sales_ops_leads.sql`; kinds `normal|conversion|lost`, `is_system`, índice único parcial por kind). Seeds `LEAD_STAGE_SEEDS` + `ensureLeadStages`/`ensureLeadStagesForOrg` em `apps/api/src/domains/sales-ops/leads/stages-seed.ts`. Leitura `listLeadStages` em `stage-service.ts:79` **não semeia**; único caller do seed em runtime é `apps/api/src/domains/import/routes.ts:52`. Por isso orgs criadas após a 0022 (ECV Control) ficam sem etapas.
- Lead do CRM: `sales_ops_leads` (+ `sales_ops_lead_products`; campos de contato da migr. 0027; soft delete 0028; `sale_id` liga à proposta). Dois contratos (`leadFieldSet`: `full`/`contact`) em `apps/api/src/domains/sales-ops/leads/lead-schemas.ts`. Serviços: `lead-service.ts`, `lead-trash-service.ts`, rotas `lead-routes.ts`, `stage-routes.ts`.
- Lead legado de indicação: tabela `leads` (`apps/api/src/db/schema.ts:233`, finder/click/link), usada em `apps/api/src/domains/conversions/service.ts`.
- Propostas/vendas: `sales_ops_sales` (`status` `draft|open|won|lost|cancelled`, schema.ts ~l.809), `_sale_items`, `_sale_professionals`; financeiro: `sales_ops_receivables`, `sales_ops_payables`, `sales_ops_settlements`; integração com Finanças por outbox (`domains/integration/*`, eventos `fxl-sales.*`).
- Web: rota única `/:workspace/:view/:saleId?` em `apps/web/src/sales-ops/navigation.ts` (listas paralelas `leadsEdition*`); `apps/web/src/sales-ops/SalesOpsApp.tsx` tem ~9.700 linhas; kanban em `apps/web/src/sales-ops/leads/*`; settlements em `apps/web/src/sales-ops/settlements/*`; importação em `apps/web/src/sales-ops/import` + `apps/api/src/domains/import/*`.
- Marca na UI: `apps/web/src/sales-ops/missing-entitlement-copy.ts`, `apps/web/src/i18n/{pt-BR,en}.json`, `apps/api/src/domains/import/template.ts`, `apps/web/index.html` (título), comentários.

## 2. Base aprovada pelo Cauê (o QUÊ)

1. **Marca:** "FXL Sales" → **"FXL Vendas"** em tudo que o usuário vê (UI, i18n, planilha modelo, título, e-mails/textos). Ids técnicos inalterados (regra 0.5).
2. **Um app só, sem edições.** Remover `full`/`leads` como conceito. Módulos por organização **no banco**: plano + feature flags (ex.: `orcamentos`, `comissoes`, `financeiro`, `finders`, `catalogo`, `importacao`). Substitui `sales.edition.leads` do token. Construbom = uma org com `orcamentos` (e dependentes) desligados. Transição: enquanto existir org com o módulo antigo no token, um backfill/compat lê o token uma vez e grava a flag (documentar; não tocar no Hub de produção).
3. **Funil como fluxograma:**
   - etapa com **tipo + comportamento** (ex.: `orcamento` abre o módulo Orçamentos; `normal`; `saida`);
   - **transições permitidas** explícitas (grafo);
   - **etapas de saída tipadas**: `ganho`, `perdido`, `pausado`;
   - **vários funis por organização** (ex.: "Novos", "Carteira");
   - **Kanban é só uma visualização** do funil;
   - org sem configuração recebe o **funil CRM padrão** (semeado de forma idempotente).
4. **Lead:** obrigatórios só **nome** e **contato**. O resto são **campos dinâmicos tipados e filtráveis**: SIMPLES (texto, número, valor, data, opções) ou REFERÊNCIA (Cliente, Contato, Produto, Serviço, Venda). **Cliente** é campo de referência padrão, **não removível**.
5. **Página do lead:** clicar abre **resumo** (não o form): etapa atual, histórico com notas, botão **Avançar** listando só as transições válidas. Ao avançar, pedir **próximo passo + data** → lista de **follow-ups**. Histórico de mudança de etapa gravado **automaticamente** (servidor).
6. **Migração sem perda:** nada de leads, etapas ou dados do modo full se perde. Tabelas `leads` (legado finder) e `sales_ops_leads` **unificadas se o levantamento permitir** (risco alto ⇒ pendência), com **teste de migração** (contagens, somas de valor, vínculos `sale_id`, soft-deletados, seller, client, stage, produtos).
7. **Componente único de lead/prospecção:** criar cliente/empresa ao digitar o nome (hoje só na edição leads: picker "Buscar ou criar novo cliente"); ao escolher a empresa, **contatos e responsável** vêm preenchidos.
8. **Proposta x vendido** separados pelo **tipo da etapa**. Todas as listas e o Kanban com **filtro por período**. Cada subitem do menu Propostas vira **lista própria** (rota própria).
9. **Bug ECV:** chamar `ensureLeadStagesForOrg` ao carregar (bootstrap e/ou `GET /lead-stages`).
10. **Segunda leva:** "dias sem comprar" (a partir da última venda ganha) e importação de planilha com **mapeamento de colunas → campos e etapas**.
11. **Fora do escopo (só listar como futuro no relatório/ROADMAP):** onboarding (configurar funis, campos, módulos), agente de IA, conexão de agenda.

## 3. Processo

- O Orquestrador (Opus 5.5) escreve um **plano abstrato** (`nexo/runs/<run-id>/00-OVERVIEW.md`) com as fases abaixo, dependências e riscos. Não escreve código.
- Para **cada fase**: Planner `sonnet` detalha slices (arquivos, testes oráculo, critérios) → plan-checker `sonnet` → Executor `haiku` em worktree `feat/<token>-<slice>` → **Verify `sonnet` separado** (oráculo + suíte afetada + lint + type-check; por wave: `pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm type-check`, `pnpm build`, integração com Postgres local) → **Code review Codex** (papel `reviewer` do Nexo, `-m gpt-6-astra high`, pela assinatura; se `agents.enabled` não estiver ligado no repo, rodar o mesmo papel com `codex exec -m gpt-6-astra` e reasoning `high` no worktree, só leitura) → **achado bloqueante ⇒ volta ao Executor `haiku`, refaz Verify `sonnet` e refaz o review** (dentro dos limites de rounds do `runtime-policy.json`; esgotou ⇒ `AUDIT.md` + pendência, sem merge) → merge `--no-ff` no `master` local → Capture (`nexo/runs/`, `nexo/knowledge/`).
- Uma fase só começa quando a anterior está verde no `master` local.
- Bloqueio que precise do Cauê ou de produção → `AUDIT.md` + pendência no relatório; seguir com o que não depende disso.
- **Migrações 100% aditivas (regra dura):** Drizzle em `apps/api/drizzle/` (próximo número após `0028_*`). **Zero `DROP`** (tabela, coluna, índice, constraint) que remova ou deixe de proteger dados, **zero `DELETE`/`TRUNCATE` de dados**, **nenhum rename destrutivo** (`RENAME`, `ALTER COLUMN ... TYPE` com perda). Só `CREATE`, `ADD COLUMN` (nullable ou com default), `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, `UPDATE` de backfill em colunas novas. Se um CHECK ou índice atual bloquear o modelo novo (ex.: 0022 — `sales_ops_lead_stages_org_kind_idx`, uma `conversion` e uma `lost` por org; `sales_ops_lead_stages_system_kind_check`), a solução tem de ser aditiva (tabelas/colunas novas onde a regra antiga não se aplica, ex.: etapas de funil novas em tabela própria, com a tabela antiga intacta) — senão **vira pendência**. Um teste/guard da wave varre as migrações novas e falha se encontrar `DROP`, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE` ou `RENAME`. Respeitar o contrato single-role (sem GRANT/ROLE em migração) e RLS por `org_id` (`FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` + policies tenant/admin como na 0022).

## 4. Fases e critérios de aceite

> **AC comum a todas as fases:** cada wave só faz merge com Verify `sonnet` verde **e** review Codex (`gpt-6-astra high`) sem achado bloqueante, ambos registrados no status/run.

### Fase 0 — Marca, bug ECV, funil padrão (pequena, primeiro)
- 0a Marca: todo texto visível "FXL Sales" → "FXL Vendas" (`missing-entitlement-copy.ts`, `i18n/*.json`, `import/template.ts` incl. `wb.creator`, `apps/web/index.html`). **AC:** grep de "FXL Sales" em `apps/web/src` + strings de UI da API = 0 (comentários podem ficar); `fxl-sales` ids intactos (teste guarda contando appId/eventos/envs/localStorage inalterados); testes de copy atualizados.
- 0b Bug ECV: `GET /lead-stages` e o bootstrap chamam `ensureLeadStagesForOrg`. **AC:** teste de integração: org nova sem etapas → após o GET tem as 4 seeds; segunda chamada não duplica; org que renomeou/arquivou etapas não recebe reinserção; concorrência (2 GETs simultâneos) não gera 500.
- 0c Funil padrão: definir o "funil CRM padrão" (proposta inicial: Novo → Qualificação → Em negociação → Proposta(orçamento) → Ganho / Perdido / Pausado) **como constante única**, ainda sobre o modelo atual se possível. **AC:** fonte única da seed, teste que compara constante e migração (como `leads-schema-migration.test.ts`).

### Fase 1 — Levantamento das entidades (documento obrigatório + Verify) → modelo de dados e migração
- **1a Levantamento crítico (só leitura, entrega um documento `nexo/runs/<run-id>/levantamento-entidades.md`)** para cada entidade: Lead (`sales_ops_leads` e `leads` legado), Etapa, Funil, Cliente/Empresa (`sales_ops_clients`), Contato (hoje campos do lead), Pessoa/Vendedor/Responsável (`sales_ops_people`, funções), Produto/Serviço (`sales_ops_products`, áreas, custos por função), Proposta/Orçamento/Venda (`sales_ops_sales`, itens, profissionais), Recebível/A pagar/Liquidação, Comissão/Finder/Indicação/Click/Conversão, Importação, Auditoria/histórico. Para cada uma: onde aparece (arquivos API e web), telas, rotas, eventos e outbox (**incluindo o que o FXL Finanças consome** via `fxl-sales.obligation.*`/`settlement.*`), RLS, testes; **o que quebra se virar campo dinâmico**; recomendação **fixo vs configurável**. **Documento obrigatório**, conferido por um **Verify `sonnet` separado** (confere cada citação contra o código e a classificação de risco) antes de qualquer modelo de dados. Cada entidade recebe risco **baixo/médio/alto**. **Risco alto ⇒ caminho conservador:** não unificar, não mudar, registrar como pendência no relatório. São risco alto por definição: unificar `leads` (legado finder) com `sales_ops_leads`; qualquer mudança nos eventos `fxl-sales.obligation.*`/`settlement.*`; a pagar e a receber (`sales_ops_payables`, `sales_ops_receivables`, `sales_ops_settlements`). Nada de modelo novo antes do Verify do levantamento passar.
- **1b Modelo de dados** (após o Verify do levantamento; só para entidades de risco baixo/médio): funis (`pipelines`), etapas com `type`/`behavior`/`exit_kind`, transições, definições de campos por org (tipo simples/referência, filtrável), valores de campos (estratégia de armazenamento e índice para filtro — justificar JSONB vs EAV), lead unificado, eventos/histórico de etapa, notas, follow-ups (próximo passo + data), módulos/flags por org. **AC:** ADR em `nexo/knowledge/decisions/` citando o levantamento; schema Drizzle + migração 100% aditiva com RLS; guard de migração aditiva verde; entidades de risco alto sem alteração e listadas como pendência.
- **1c Migração de dados** (cópia aditiva; origens intactas): backfill de `sales_ops_leads` para o lead novo — `leads` (legado finder) **só entra se o levantamento não a classificar como risco alto** (por definição é; então fica como pendência, sem tocar); etapas atuais → funil "Padrão" de cada org (`conversion` → tipo orçamento/proposta; `lost` → saída perdido); `stage_changed_at` vira primeiro evento de histórico; Cliente vira valor do campo de referência; produtos do lead viram campo referência Produto. **AC:** teste de migração com dataset sintético isolado cobrindo os dois modos (org full com propostas ganhas, perdidas, leads convertidos, soft-deletados, sem vendedor; org estilo Construbom sem produtos; org sem etapas; leads legados de finder com click/link/conversion) — **todas as tabelas de origem com contagem e conteúdo idênticos antes/depois** (nada apagado/alterado), contagens e somas do destino batendo com a origem, payables/receivables/settlements e outbox inalterados, nenhum FK órfão, RLS isolando orgs, rodar migração 2x é idempotente. Leitura antiga continua funcionando (compat) até a Fase 2.

### Fase 2 — Componente e página do lead
- Componente único de criação/edição (substitui os dois contratos `full`/`contact`): criar empresa ao digitar; ao escolher empresa preenche contatos e responsável; campos dinâmicos renderizados por tipo.
- Página de resumo do lead (nova rota, ex.: `/operacional/leads/:leadId`): etapa atual, histórico + notas, botão Avançar com só as transições válidas, modal de próximo passo + data; lista de follow-ups (minha/da equipe, filtro por período).
- Kanban vira visualização do funil selecionado (seletor de funil); Proposta x Vendido separados pelo tipo da etapa; filtro por período em todas as listas e no Kanban; cada subitem do menu Propostas com rota/lista própria (atualizar `navigation.ts`, sem listas paralelas por edição).
- **AC:** testes de componente (web vitest) e de API: transição inválida → 4xx; avançar grava evento de histórico no servidor; follow-up obrigatório com data; Cliente não removível; filtros por período corretos com fuso America/Sao_Paulo; clicar no card abre o resumo, não o form.

### Fase 3 — Módulos por organização
- Tabela de plano/flags por org; `require-capability.ts` passa a ler do banco (cache por request); `resolveSalesEdition` e `SALES_EDITION_LEADS_MODULE` deixam de decidir produto (manter compat de leitura do token só para backfill inicial, documentado). Remover `leadsEdition*` de `navigation.ts` em favor de filtro por módulo. Etapa tipo orçamento só aparece/age se módulo Orçamentos ativo.
- **AC:** testes: org com orçamentos off (Construbom) vê exatamente o que vê hoje + novidades da Fase 2; org full sem regressão (suíte inteira verde); toggle de flag muda navegação e 403 `edition_capability` (ou novo código equivalente, documentado) sem redeploy; nenhum teste de guard (`no-legacy-*`, `hub-sdk-pin`, `fxl-contracts-pin`) quebrado.

### Fase 4 — Segunda leva
- "Dias sem comprar" por cliente = hoje − data da última venda **ganha** (etapa de saída ganho / `sales_ops_sales.status='won'`), exibido e filtrável. **AC:** teste com cliente sem venda (vazio, não 0), com várias vendas, com venda perdida ignorada.
- Importação com mapeamento de colunas → campos dinâmicos e etapas/funis (evolui `apps/api/src/domains/import/*` e `apps/web/src/sales-ops/import`), com preview. **AC:** preview + commit idempotentes; reconhecimento de cliente existente mantido; planilha modelo atualizada com marca FXL Vendas.

## 5. Relatório final (obrigatório)
1. O que entrou por fase, commits no `master` local, worktrees/branches criados (todos mantidos).
2. **Modelo efetivo por papel** (orquestrador, planner, plan-checker, executor, verify, reviewer Codex, scribe), lido dos registros. **Confirmar, wave por wave, que o review do Codex rodou**, com o modelo (`gpt-6-astra high`), o modo de autenticação (assinatura), o veredito e os rounds de correção.
3. Resultado de cada Gate 2 / wave-verify (números de testes).
4. **Pendências:** entidades de risco alto não alteradas (com o motivo do levantamento), soluções aditivas não encontradas; **dados reais:** impacto da migração em produção (volumes, tempo, ordem web/API, rollback), backfill das flags a partir do Hub, eventual limpeza futura de tabelas/colunas antigas (só com decisão do Cauê), ativação/validação com Construbom e ECV Control — tudo **não executado**.
5. Futuro (não feito): onboarding de funis/campos/módulos, agente de IA, conexão de agenda.
6. Lembrete: produção só via `/nexo-ship` com OK explícito do Cauê.

 succeeded in 0ms:
-- 0031_crm_cutover_resync - the phase 2 cutover RESYNC (vendas-crm-unificado slice 07a1).
--
-- Part 1, statements 1 to 17: a BYTE-FOR-BYTE repeat of 0030_crm_legacy_copy.sql, from the first
-- set_config to the last INSERT (crm-cutover-resync.test.ts compares the two files). It copies what
-- the old tables received after 0030 and is a no-op for what is already there (same uuids,
-- ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING).
--
-- Part 2, after the repeat: what a repeat cannot do. A row that was changed in the OLD tables after
-- 0030 (a lead moved, edited, deleted or restored, an etapa renamed or archived) keeps a stale copy,
-- so the rows the old table changed LAST are written over the copy. "Changed last" is the rule
-- COALESCE(old.updated_at, '-infinity') > COALESCE(new.updated_at, '-infinity'): every old writer
-- (create, patch, move, delete, restore, etapa edit) stamps updated_at, and the new code always
-- stamps it too, so a row the NEW code wrote is never overwritten and the whole part 2 is idempotent
-- (a second run changes nothing).
--   * etapas: name, position, status, archived_at, updated_at (a rename that would collide with
--     another etapa of the pipeline is skipped, never aborts the migration);
--   * one history event (from the copy's etapa to the old etapa, actor 'system'/'Migração') for every
--     lead whose etapa differs; written BEFORE the lead update so the comparison still sees both;
--   * leads: contact, etapa, position, stage_changed_at, vendedor, empresa, valor, lost_reason, sale_id,
--     deleted_* and updated_at;
--   * field values: descricao, nascimento and produtos are upserted (ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE) from
--     the changed leads. A value row that was REMOVED in the old table (a produto dropped, a
--     description cleared, a birth date cleared) is NOT removed here: a migration may not DELETE.
--     The stale value stays visible until an operator edits the lead. Accepted, documented, tested.
--   * transitions: a stage created in the old table after 0030 gets its edges (the 0030 rule, restricted to the
--     pairs that touch a stage with NO edge yet, so a graph the org shaped is left alone);
--
-- After this migration the API reads and writes ONLY the unified tables (slice 07a2): the old
-- tables sales_ops_leads, sales_ops_lead_products and sales_ops_lead_stages stay byte-intact as the
-- frozen pre-cutover copy and are never written again by product code. 0030 and 0031 ship in the
-- same deploy, so the window this resync covers is empty in practice.
-- 100% additive to the schema: INSERT and UPDATE of rows of the unified tables only, no DELETE, no
-- privilege statement, ordinary non-phased migration.
SELECT set_config('app.fxl_admin', 'true', true);--> statement-breakpoint
INSERT INTO "sales_ops_pipelines" ("org_id", "name", "system_key", "position", "is_default")
SELECT o."org_id", 'Funil CRM padrão', 'crm-padrao', 0, true
FROM (
  SELECT "org_id" FROM "sales_ops_people"
  UNION SELECT "org_id" FROM "sales_ops_settings"
  UNION SELECT "org_id" FROM "sales_ops_sales"
  UNION SELECT "org_id" FROM "sales_ops_lead_stages"
  UNION SELECT "org_id" FROM "sales_ops_leads"
  UNION SELECT "org_id" FROM "sales_ops_clients"
) AS o
ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING;--> statement-breakpoint
INSERT INTO "sales_ops_pipeline_stages" ("id", "org_id", "pipeline_id", "name", "position", "type", "behavior", "exit_kind", "requires_reason", "status", "archived_at", "created_at", "updated_at")
SELECT s."id", s."org_id", p."id", s."name", s."position",
  CASE s."kind" WHEN 'conversion' THEN 'orcamento' WHEN 'lost' THEN 'saida' ELSE 'normal' END,
  CASE s."kind" WHEN 'conversion' THEN 'opens_orcamentos' ELSE 'none' END,
  CASE s."kind" WHEN 'lost' THEN 'perdido' ELSE NULL END,
  s."kind" = 'lost',

import { isIsoDay, saoPauloDayRangeToInstants } from '@fxl-sales/shared-utils/sao-paulo-day';
import { eq, gte, lt, sql, type SQL } from 'drizzle-orm';
import type { getDb } from '../../../db/client.js';
import { salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues, salesOpsCrmLeads } from '../../../db/schema.js';
import { loadFieldDefinitions, type FieldDefinitionRow } from '../../crm/lead-fields.js';
import { defaultPipelineId } from '../../crm/pipeline-read.js';
import type { DynamicFilterInput } from './lead-schemas.js';

/**
 * The board read narrowing added by phase 2: pipeline, period (America/Sao_Paulo civil days over
 * `created_at`) and dynamic filters on FILTERABLE field definitions. One builder serves
 * `listLeads` and `summarizeLeadStages`, so a column's total and the summary's count always cover
 * the same set.
 */
type Db = ReturnType<typeof getDb>;

/** A filter key that is unknown, not filterable, or whose value does not fit its kind. The route answers 400. */
export class LeadFilterError extends Error {
  constructor(readonly filter: string) {
    super(`invalid_filter:${filter}`);
    this.name = 'LeadFilterError';
  }
}

const FILTER_KEY = /^f\.([a-z0-9_-]{1,60})(?:\.(min|max))?$/i;
const UUID = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;

/** Reads every `f.<key>` / `f.<key>.min` / `f.<key>.max` entry of a query string. Any other `f.` shape is a 400. */
export function parseDynamicFilters(query: Record<string, string>): DynamicFilterInput[] {
  const filters: DynamicFilterInput[] = [];
  for (const [name, raw] of Object.entries(query)) {
    if (!name.startsWith('f.')) continue;
    const match = FILTER_KEY.exec(name);
    if (!match) throw new LeadFilterError(name);
    filters.push({ key: match[1]!, op: (match[2]?.toLowerCase() as 'min' | 'max' | undefined) ?? 'eq', raw });
  }
  return filters;
}

function valueExists(def: FieldDefinitionRow, predicate: SQL): SQL {
  return sql`EXISTS (
    SELECT 1 FROM ${salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues} v
    WHERE v.org_id = ${salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId} AND v.lead_id = ${salesOpsCrmLeads.id}
      AND v.field_id = ${def.id} AND ${predicate})`;
}

function escapeLike(raw: string): string {
  return raw.replace(/[\\%_]/g, (char) => `\\${char}`);
}

function condition(def: FieldDefinitionRow, filter: DynamicFilterInput): SQL {
  const bad = () => new LeadFilterError(`f.${filter.key}`);
  const range = filter.op !== 'eq';
  const raw = filter.raw.trim();
  if (raw === '') throw bad();
  if (def.backingColumn === 'client_id') {
    if (range || !UUID.test(raw)) throw bad();
    return eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.clientId, raw);
  }
  switch (def.kind) {
    case 'text':
      if (range) throw bad();
      return valueExists(def, sql`v.value_text ILIKE ${`%${escapeLike(raw)}%`} ESCAPE '\\'`);
    case 'options':
      if (range) throw bad();
      return valueExists(def, sql`v.value_option = ${raw}`);
    case 'number':
    case 'money': {
      const n = Number(raw);
      if (!Number.isFinite(n) || (def.kind === 'money' && !Number.isSafeInteger(n))) throw bad();
      const column = def.kind === 'number' ? sql`v.value_number` : sql`v.value_cents`;
      if (filter.op === 'min') return valueExists(def, sql`${column} >= ${n}`);
      if (filter.op === 'max') return valueExists(def, sql`${column} <= ${n}`);
      return valueExists(def, sql`${column} = ${n}`);
    }
    case 'date': {
      if (!isIsoDay(raw)) throw bad();
      if (filter.op === 'min') return valueExists(def, sql`v.value_date >= ${raw}::date`);
      if (filter.op === 'max') return valueExists(def, sql`v.value_date <= ${raw}::date`);
      return valueExists(def, sql`v.value_date = ${raw}::date`);
    }
    case 'reference': {
      if (range || !UUID.test(raw)) throw bad();
      switch (def.referenceTarget) {
        case 'cliente':
          return valueExists(def, sql`v.value_client_id = ${raw}::uuid`);
        case 'produto':
        case 'servico':
          return valueExists(def, sql`v.value_product_id = ${raw}::uuid`);
        case 'venda':
          return valueExists(def, sql`v.value_sale_id = ${raw}::uuid`);
        default:
          throw bad();
      }
    }
    default:
      throw bad();
  }
}

export type BoardNarrowing = {
  pipelineId?: string | undefined;
  from?: string | undefined;
  to?: string | undefined;
  filters?: DynamicFilterInput[] | undefined;
};

/**
 * The extra WHERE conjuncts of a board read. `defaultToDefaultPipeline` is true for the summary,
 * which has no stageId: it then covers the org's default pipeline unless one is named.
 */
export async function boardNarrowingConditions(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  narrowing: BoardNarrowing,
  options: { defaultToDefaultPipeline: boolean },
): Promise<SQL[]> {
  const conditions: SQL[] = [];
  const pipelineId =
    narrowing.pipelineId ?? (options.defaultToDefaultPipeline ? await defaultPipelineId(tx, orgId) : null);
  if (pipelineId) conditions.push(eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.pipelineId, pipelineId));

  const { start, endExclusive } = saoPauloDayRangeToInstants(narrowing.from, narrowing.to);
  if (start) conditions.push(gte(salesOpsCrmLeads.createdAt, start));
  if (endExclusive) conditions.push(lt(salesOpsCrmLeads.createdAt, endExclusive));

  if (narrowing.filters && narrowing.filters.length > 0) {
    const definitions = (await loadFieldDefinitions(tx, orgId)).filter((def) => def.status === 'active');
    for (const filter of narrowing.filters) {
      const def = definitions.find((candidate) => candidate.key === filter.key);
      if (!def || !def.filterable) throw new LeadFilterError(`f.${filter.key}`);
      conditions.push(condition(def, filter));
    }
  }
  return conditions;
}
import { isIsoDay } from '@fxl-sales/shared-utils/sao-paulo-day';
import { and, asc, eq, inArray, sql } from 'drizzle-orm';
import type { getDb } from '../../db/client.js';
import {
  salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues,
  salesOpsFieldDefinitions,
  salesOpsProducts,
  salesOpsSales,
} from '../../db/schema.js';
import { LeadInputError } from './lead-input-error.js';

/**
 * Typed dynamic lead fields (EAV) for the unified CRM lead: read them as the wire shape, validate a
 * write against the field definition, store each value in exactly one typed slot.
 *
 * The slot rule is the SEAM-CONTRACT's: text -> value_text; number -> value_number; money ->
 * value_cents (integer cents); date -> value_date; options -> value_option (the option key);
 * reference cliente -> value_client_id; produto or servico -> value_product_id + snapshot, OR
 * value_text + snapshot for a FREE item; venda -> value_sale_id; contato -> value_text.
 * The Cliente system field is NOT stored here: its `backing_column` is the lead's `client_id`, so
 * a write to it is handed back to the caller as `cliente` and read from the lead row.
 *
 * Pure of request context: takes the tenant transaction its caller already opened.
 */
type Db = ReturnType<typeof getDb>;

export type FieldDefinitionRow = typeof salesOpsFieldDefinitions.$inferSelect;
type ValueInsert = typeof salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues.$inferInsert;

export type FieldWireItem = { id: string | null; name: string };
export type FieldWireValue = string | number | null | FieldWireItem[];
export type LeadFieldValue = { fieldId: string; key: string; value: FieldWireValue };

/** One `fields[]` entry of a write body (already shape-checked by `LeadFieldWriteSchema`). */
export type FieldWriteInput = {
  fieldId?: string | undefined;
  key?: string | undefined;
  value: string | number | null | Array<{ id?: string | null | undefined; name?: string | undefined }>;
};

/** The definitions the legacy write keys map onto. Byte-equal to the rows migration 0030/0031 creates. */
export const LEGACY_FIELD_SPECS = {
  produtos: {
    key: 'produtos',
    label: 'Produtos',
    kind: 'reference',
    referenceTarget: 'produto',
    multiple: true,
    filterable: true,
    position: 2,
  },
  descricao: { key: 'descricao', label: 'Descrição', kind: 'text', filterable: false, position: 3 },
  nascimento: { key: 'nascimento', label: 'Nascimento', kind: 'date', filterable: false, position: 4 },
} as const;
export type LegacyFieldKey = keyof typeof LEGACY_FIELD_SPECS;

export function loadFieldDefinitions(tx: Db, orgId: string): Promise<FieldDefinitionRow[]> {
  return tx
    .select()
    .from(salesOpsFieldDefinitions)
    .where(eq(salesOpsFieldDefinitions.orgId, orgId))
    .orderBy(asc(salesOpsFieldDefinitions.position), asc(salesOpsFieldDefinitions.key));
}

/**
 * The definition behind a legacy write key (`description`, `contactBirthDate`, `products`),
 * created on first use. A bare ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING on the unique (org_id, key) index, then a
 * re-read, so two concurrent first writes never 500.
 */
export async function ensureLegacyFieldDefinition(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  key: LegacyFieldKey,
): Promise<FieldDefinitionRow> {
  await tx
    .insert(salesOpsFieldDefinitions)
    .values({ orgId, ...LEGACY_FIELD_SPECS[key] })
    .onConflictDoNothing();
  const [row] = await tx
    .select()
    .from(salesOpsFieldDefinitions)
    .where(and(eq(salesOpsFieldDefinitions.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsFieldDefinitions.key, key)))
    .limit(1);
  if (!row) throw new Error(`field definition ${key} disappeared inside its own transaction`);
  return row;
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Reads
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

type ValueRow = typeof salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues.$inferSelect;

function rowToItem(def: FieldDefinitionRow, row: ValueRow): FieldWireItem {
  switch (def.referenceTarget) {
    case 'cliente':
      return { id: row.valueClientId, name: row.refNameSnapshot ?? '' };
    case 'produto':
    case 'servico':
      return { id: row.valueProductId, name: row.refNameSnapshot ?? row.valueText ?? '' };
    case 'venda':
      return { id: row.valueSaleId, name: row.refNameSnapshot ?? '' };
    default:
      return { id: null, name: row.valueText ?? '' };
  }
}

function rowsToWire(def: FieldDefinitionRow, rows: ValueRow[]): FieldWireValue {
  if (rows.length === 0) return null;
  switch (def.kind) {
    case 'number':
      return rows[0]!.valueNumber === null ? null : Number(rows[0]!.valueNumber);
    case 'money':
      return rows[0]!.valueCents;
    case 'date':
      return rows[0]!.valueDate;
    case 'options':
      return rows[0]!.valueOption;
    case 'reference':
      return rows.map((row) => rowToItem(def, row));
    default:
      return rows[0]!.valueText;
  }
}

export type FieldReadLead = { id: string; clientId: string | null; clientNameSnapshot: string };

/**
 * Every ACTIVE definition for every lead, in definition order, `null` when the lead has no value.
 * Two statements regardless of the page size: the definitions, then every value of the page.
 */
export async function readLeadFields(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  leads: readonly FieldReadLead[],
): Promise<Map<string, LeadFieldValue[]>> {
  const byLead = new Map<string, LeadFieldValue[]>();
  if (leads.length === 0) return byLead;
  const definitions = (await loadFieldDefinitions(tx, orgId)).filter((def) => def.status === 'active');
  const values = await tx
    .select()
    .from(salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues)
    .where(
      and(
        eq(salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues.orgId, orgId),
        inArray(
          salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues.leadId,
          leads.map((lead) => lead.id),
        ),
      ),
    )
    .orderBy(asc(salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues.fieldId), asc(salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues.position));
  const rowsByLeadField = new Map<string, ValueRow[]>();
  for (const row of values) {
    const key = `${row.leadId}:${row.fieldId}`;
    const bucket = rowsByLeadField.get(key) ?? [];
    bucket.push(row);
    rowsByLeadField.set(key, bucket);
  }
  for (const lead of leads) {
    byLead.set(
      lead.id,
      definitions.map((def) => {
        if (def.backingColumn === 'client_id') {
          return {
            fieldId: def.id,
            key: def.key,
            value: lead.clientId ? [{ id: lead.clientId, name: lead.clientNameSnapshot }] : null,
          };
        }
        return { fieldId: def.id, key: def.key, value: rowsToWire(def, rowsByLeadField.get(`${lead.id}:${def.id}`) ?? []) };
      }),
    );
  }
  return byLead;
}

/** The legacy projection of a lead's fields: `description`, `contactBirthDate` and `products` keep their old keys. */
export function projectLegacyFields(fields: readonly LeadFieldValue[]): {
  description: string | null;
  contactBirthDate: string | null;
  products: Array<{ productId: string | null; productNameSnapshot: string }>;
} {
  const of = (key: string) => fields.find((field) => field.key === key)?.value ?? null;
  const description = of('descricao');
  const birth = of('nascimento');
  const products = of('produtos');
  return {
    description: typeof description === 'string' ? description : null,
    contactBirthDate: typeof birth === 'string' ? birth : null,
    // Displayed by SNAPSHOT name (then id), the pre-cutover order: the stored positions are the write
    // order, which is not a list the operator authored.
    products: Array.isArray(products)
      ? products
          .map((item) => ({ productId: item.id, productNameSnapshot: item.name }))
          .sort(
            (a, b) =>
              a.productNameSnapshot.localeCompare(b.productNameSnapshot, 'en') ||
              (a.productId ?? '').localeCompare(b.productId ?? ''),
          )
      : [],
  };
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Writes
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/** A validated replacement of ONE field's value set; empty `rows` clears the field. */
export type ResolvedFieldWrite = { definition: FieldDefinitionRow; rows: Array<Partial<ValueInsert>> };

export type ResolvedFieldWrites = {
  writes: ResolvedFieldWrite[];
  /** Set when the body wrote the Cliente field: the caller applies it to the lead's client_id. */
  cliente: { clientId: string | null; index: number } | null;
};

const invalid = (index: number) => new LeadInputError('field_invalid', index);
const MAX_TEXT = 4000;
const MAX_NUMBER = 1e13;

type RefItem = { id?: string | null | undefined; name?: string | undefined };

async function resolveProductItems(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  items: readonly RefItem[],
  missing: (index: number) => LeadInputError,
): Promise<Array<Partial<ValueInsert>>> {
  const rows: Array<Partial<ValueInsert>> = [];
  for (const [index, item] of items.entries()) {
    if (!item.id) {
      const name = item.name?.trim() ?? '';
      if (name === '' || name.length > 140) throw missing(index);
      rows.push({ valueText: name, refNameSnapshot: name });
      continue;
    }
    const [product] = await tx
      .select({ id: salesOpsProducts.id, name: salesOpsProducts.name })
      .from(salesOpsProducts)
      .where(and(eq(salesOpsProducts.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsProducts.id, item.id)))
      .limit(1);
    if (!product) throw missing(index);
    rows.push({ valueProductId: product.id, refNameSnapshot: product.name });
  }
  return rows;
}

async function resolveReference(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  def: FieldDefinitionRow,
  items: readonly RefItem[],
  index: number,
): Promise<Array<Partial<ValueInsert>>> {
  const notFound = () => new LeadInputError('field_reference_not_found', index);
  switch (def.referenceTarget) {
    case 'produto':
    case 'servico':
      return resolveProductItems(tx, orgId, items, notFound);
    case 'venda': {
      const rows: Array<Partial<ValueInsert>> = [];
      for (const item of items) {
        if (!item.id) throw notFound();
        const [sale] = await tx
          .select({ id: salesOpsSales.id, code: salesOpsSales.code })
          .from(salesOpsSales)
          .where(and(eq(salesOpsSales.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsSales.id, item.id)))
          .limit(1);
        if (!sale) throw notFound();
        rows.push({ valueSaleId: sale.id, refNameSnapshot: sale.code });
      }
      return rows;
    }
    case 'contato':
      return items.map((item) => {
        const name = item.name?.trim() ?? '';
        if (name === '' || name.length > 200) throw invalid(index);
        return { valueText: name, refNameSnapshot: name };
      });
    case 'cliente': {
      const rows: Array<Partial<ValueInsert>> = [];
      for (const item of items) {
        if (!item.id) throw notFound();
        const found = (await tx.execute(
          sql`SELECT name FROM sales_ops_clients WHERE org_id = ${orgId} AND id = ${item.id}::uuid LIMIT 1`,
        )) as unknown as Array<{ name: string }>;
        if (found[0] === undefined) throw notFound();
        rows.push({ valueClientId: item.id, refNameSnapshot: found[0].name });
      }
      return rows;
    }
    default:
      throw invalid(index);
  }
}

async function resolveOne(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  def: FieldDefinitionRow,
  value: FieldWriteInput['value'],
  index: number,
): Promise<Array<Partial<ValueInsert>>> {
  if (value === null) return [];
  switch (def.kind) {
    case 'text': {
      if (typeof value !== 'string') throw invalid(index);
      const text = value.trim();
      if (text.length > MAX_TEXT) throw invalid(index);
      return text === '' ? [] : [{ valueText: text }];
    }
    case 'number': {
      if (typeof value !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(value) || Math.abs(value) >= MAX_NUMBER) throw invalid(index);
      return [{ valueNumber: String(value) }];
    }
    case 'money': {
      if (typeof value !== 'number' || !Number.isSafeInteger(value) || value < 0) throw invalid(index);
      return [{ valueCents: value }];
    }
    case 'date': {
      if (typeof value !== 'string' || !isIsoDay(value)) throw invalid(index);
      return [{ valueDate: value }];
    }
    case 'options': {
      const options = Array.isArray(def.options) ? (def.options as Array<{ key?: unknown }>) : [];
      if (typeof value !== 'string' || !options.some((option) => option.key === value)) throw invalid(index);
      return [{ valueOption: value }];
    }
    case 'reference': {
      if (!Array.isArray(value)) throw invalid(index);
      if (!def.multiple && value.length > 1) throw invalid(index);
      return resolveReference(tx, orgId, def, value, index);
    }
    default:
      throw invalid(index);
  }
}

/**
 * Validates every `fields[]` entry against its definition and returns the replacement sets.
 * Unknown or archived field -> `field_not_found`; a value of the wrong shape, a duplicate field in
 * one body, or more than one value on a single-valued field -> `field_invalid`; a reference that
 * does not exist in the org -> `field_reference_not_found`. All carry the entry index.
 */
export async function resolveFieldWrites(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  inputs: readonly FieldWriteInput[],
): Promise<ResolvedFieldWrites> {
  const result: ResolvedFieldWrites = { writes: [], cliente: null };
  if (inputs.length === 0) return result;
  const definitions = (await loadFieldDefinitions(tx, orgId)).filter((def) => def.status === 'active');
  const seen = new Set<string>();
  for (const [index, input] of inputs.entries()) {
    const def = definitions.find((candidate) =>
      input.fieldId ? candidate.id === input.fieldId : candidate.key === input.key,
    );
    if (!def) throw new LeadInputError('field_not_found', index);
    if (seen.has(def.id)) throw invalid(index);
    seen.add(def.id);
    if (def.backingColumn === 'client_id') {
      if (input.value === null) {
        result.cliente = { clientId: null, index };
        continue;
      }
      if (!Array.isArray(input.value) || input.value.length !== 1 || !input.value[0]!.id) throw invalid(index);
      result.cliente = { clientId: input.value[0]!.id, index };
      continue;
    }
    result.writes.push({ definition: def, rows: await resolveOne(tx, orgId, def, input.value, index) });
  }
  return result;
}

/** Replaces each field's value set: delete the lead's rows of that field, insert the new ones at positions 0..n. */
export async function applyFieldWrites(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  leadId: string,
  writes: readonly ResolvedFieldWrite[],
): Promise<void> {
  for (const write of writes) {
    await tx
      .delete(salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues)
      .where(
        and(
          eq(salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues.orgId, orgId),
          eq(salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues.leadId, leadId),
          eq(salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues.fieldId, write.definition.id),
        ),
      );
    if (write.rows.length === 0) continue;
    await tx.insert(salesOpsCrmLeadFieldValues).values(
      write.rows.map((row, position) => ({ ...row, orgId, leadId, fieldId: write.definition.id, position })),
    );
  }
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Legacy write keys
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/** The old lead payload keys, mapped onto their field definitions. `undefined` means "not in the body". */
export type LegacyFieldInput = {
  description?: string | null | undefined;
  contactBirthDate?: string | null | undefined;
  products?: ReadonlyArray<{ productId?: string | undefined; productName?: string | undefined }> | undefined;
};

async function existingDefinition(tx: Db, orgId: string, key: LegacyFieldKey): Promise<FieldDefinitionRow | null> {
  const [row] = await tx
    .select()
    .from(salesOpsFieldDefinitions)
    .where(and(eq(salesOpsFieldDefinitions.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsFieldDefinitions.key, key)))
    .limit(1);
  return row ?? null;
}

/**
 * `description` -> `descricao`, `contactBirthDate` -> `nascimento`, `products` -> `produtos`.
 * A non-empty value creates the definition on first use; clearing writes nothing when the org
 * never had the definition (there is nothing to clear).
 */
export async function resolveLegacyFieldWrites(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  input: LegacyFieldInput,
): Promise<ResolvedFieldWrite[]> {
  const writes: ResolvedFieldWrite[] = [];
  const scalar = async (key: LegacyFieldKey, value: string | null, slot: 'valueText' | 'valueDate') => {
    const text = value === null ? '' : value.trim();
    if (text === '') {
      const existing = await existingDefinition(tx, orgId, key);
      if (existing) writes.push({ definition: existing, rows: [] });
      return;
    }
    writes.push({ definition: await ensureLegacyFieldDefinition(tx, orgId, key), rows: [{ [slot]: text }] });
  };
  if (input.description !== undefined) await scalar('descricao', input.description, 'valueText');
  if (input.contactBirthDate !== undefined) await scalar('nascimento', input.contactBirthDate, 'valueDate');
  if (input.products !== undefined) {
    const items = input.products.map((row) => ({ id: row.productId ?? null, name: row.productName }));
    const rows = await resolveProductItems(tx, orgId, items, (index) => new LeadInputError('product_not_found', index));
    if (rows.length === 0) {
      const existing = await existingDefinition(tx, orgId, 'produtos');
      if (existing) writes.push({ definition: existing, rows: [] });
    } else {
      writes.push({ definition: await ensureLegacyFieldDefinition(tx, orgId, 'produtos'), rows });
    }
  }
  return writes;
}

# SEAM CONTRACT - phase 2 API and web (orchestrator)

Authoritative for the phase 2, 3 and 4 planners, on top of `contracts/SEAM-CONTRACT.md` (tables, constants, services from slices 05a, 05b, 06).
The API planner of phase 2 finalizes the exact zod schemas and wire types in its plans and must keep every name below; a deviation it cannot avoid is written in its result summary and reconciled by the orchestrator at plan-check.
All routes live under the existing `/api/v1/sales-ops` router, behind the existing access gate, org from `c.get('orgId')` only, seller scope through the one server predicate (port of `leadSellerCondition`) and `liveLeadCondition`.

## Cutover (API, code only)

- After phase 2 every lead read and write uses the NEW tables (`sales_ops_crm_leads` and friends) and the pipeline stages; nothing writes `sales_ops_leads`, `sales_ops_lead_products` or `sales_ops_lead_stages` anymore, and they stay byte-intact as the frozen pre-cutover copy.
- Migration `0031_crm_cutover_resync` repeats `0030_crm_legacy_copy.sql` verbatim (idempotent copy with the same uuids) so rows written to the old tables after 0030 are not lost.
- Existing routes keep their paths and their response shapes (additive fields only), so today's web keeps working while the web slices land: `GET/POST /leads`, `GET /leads/summary`, `GET /leads/deleted`, `GET/PATCH /leads/:id`, `POST /leads/:id/move`, `POST /leads/:id/delete`, `POST /leads/:id/restore`, `GET /lead-stages`, `POST /lead-stages`, `PATCH /lead-stages/:id`, `POST /lead-stages/reorder`, the import routes, and the lead conversion through `POST /sales` (which sets the lead's `sale_id`).
- `GET /lead-stages` returns the stages of the org's DEFAULT pipeline (or of `?pipelineId=`), each with the legacy `kind` derived as: `type = 'orcamento'` -> `'conversion'`; `exit_kind = 'perdido'` -> `'lost'`; everything else -> `'normal'`; plus the additive fields `pipelineId`, `type`, `behavior`, `exitKind`, `requiresReason`.
- `ensureCrmDefaultsForOrg` is called where `ensureLeadStagesForOrg` is called today (bootstrap, `GET /lead-stages`) and by `GET /pipelines`; registered in `crm-seed-callers.test.ts`.
- One lock order: every writer of `stage_id`/`position` on `sales_ops_crm_leads` takes the per-org board advisory lock first (same key as `lockLeadBoard`).
- The two write contracts (`full` and `contact`) collapse into ONE lead schema (`LeadWriteSchema`): `contactName` required, a contact (`contactPhone` or `contactEmail`, at least one) required, everything else optional: `pipelineId`, `stageId`, `sellerPersonId`, `clientId` or `clientName` (inline create stays a separate `POST /clients`), `estimatedValueBrl`, `fields` (dynamic values). `leadFieldSet` and the edition no longer choose a schema. Old payload keys (`description`, `contactBirthDate`, `products`) are still accepted and mapped onto their field definitions (`descricao`, `nascimento`, `produtos`) so today's web keeps working.
  Note for the planner: today the contact is optional; the request makes "nome e contato" the only required fields, so the new rule is at least one of phone or e-mail. Existing rows without contact stay valid (the rule applies to writes that touch the contact or create a lead).

## Lead wire shape (additive)

`SalesOpsLead` keeps every current field and adds `pipelineId: string`, `stageType: 'normal' | 'orcamento' | 'saida'`, `stageExitKind: 'ganho' | 'perdido' | 'pausado' | null`, `fields: LeadFieldValue[]` where `LeadFieldValue = { fieldId: string; key: string; value: string | number | null | Array<{ id: string | null; name: string }> }` (money in integer cents, date as ISO civil day, option as option key, references as `{id, name}` items).
`description`, `contactBirthDate`, `products` keep being projected from the `descricao`, `nascimento`, `produtos` field values.

## New routes

- `GET /pipelines` -> `{ pipelines: Array<{ id, name, isDefault, position, status, stages: Array<{ id, name, position, type, behavior, exitKind, requiresReason, status }>, transitions: Array<{ fromStageId, toStageId }> }> }`, active ones, ordered by position.
- `GET /field-definitions` -> `{ fields: Array<{ id, key, label, kind, referenceTarget, multiple, options, filterable, isSystem, removable, position }> }` (active). No create/edit of definitions in this run (onboarding is out of scope); the Cliente system field is always present and can never be removed (a write that omits or clears the Cliente DEFINITION does not exist; a lead may still have no cliente value).
- `GET /leads/:id/summary` -> `{ lead: SalesOpsLead, pipeline: { id, name }, stage: { id, name, type, exitKind }, nextStages: Array<{ id, name, type, exitKind, requiresReason }>, timeline: Array<{ kind: 'stage_changed' | 'note' | 'followup', at, actorName, fromStageName?, toStageName?, reason?, body?, nextStep?, dueOn?, doneAt? }>, followups: Array<{ id, nextStep, dueOn, ownerName, doneAt }> }`. `nextStages` lists ONLY the stages allowed by `sales_ops_pipeline_transitions` from the current stage.
- `POST /leads/:id/advance` body `{ toStageId, nextStep?, dueOn?, note?, reason? }`:
  - `409 { error: 'transition_not_allowed' }` when the pair is not in the transitions table (also when the stage belongs to another pipeline);
  - `400 { error: 'validation_error', reason: 'followup_required' }` when the destination is NOT an exit stage and `nextStep` or `dueOn` is missing (a follow-up is mandatory with a date); exits (`saida`) do not require one;
  - `400 ... reason: 'reason_required'` when the destination `requires_reason` and `reason` is empty;
  - writes, in ONE transaction after the board lock: the stage move (position at the end of the destination column, `stage_changed_at = now()`), exactly one `sales_ops_crm_lead_events` row (`stage_changed`, from, to, reason, actor name from the token), the note (linked to the event) when given, and the follow-up when given;
  - a destination of type `orcamento` with behaviour `opens_orcamentos` answers `409 { error: 'sale_required_for_conversion' }` exactly like today's move, because the proposta wizard owns that conversion (unchanged flow through `POST /sales`).
- `POST /leads/:id/move` (drag) enforces the same transition graph for a cross-stage move (`409 transition_not_allowed`) and writes the same history event; a reorder inside one column writes no event and never moves `stage_changed_at`.
- `POST /leads/:id/notes` `{ body }` -> 201; `GET /followups?scope=mine|team&from=YYYY-MM-DD&to=YYYY-MM-DD&status=open|done|all` (team needs admin; mine = owner is the caller's pessoa or created by the caller); `POST /leads/:id/followups` `{ nextStep, dueOn, ownerPersonId? }`; `POST /followups/:id/done`.
- Period filter on every list: `from`/`to` ISO civil days interpreted in `America/Sao_Paulo` (inclusive), validated with `isIsoDay`; leads filter on `created_at`, follow-ups on `due_on`, propostas on their existing date semantics (created for open ones, `won_at` for won ones). Shared helper converts a civil-day range into `[from 00:00 -03:00, to+1 00:00 -03:00)` through `@fxl-sales/shared-utils/sao-paulo-day`; never `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)`.
- `GET /leads` and `GET /leads/summary` accept `pipelineId` (default: the org's default pipeline), `from`, `to`, and dynamic filters `f.<fieldKey>=<value>` for `filterable` fields only (cliente by id, options by key, text by case-insensitive contains, number/money/date by `min`/`max` as `f.<key>.min`); an unknown or non-filterable key is `400`.

## Web (consumes the routes above; no edition branch for these features)

- ONE lead component `LeadForm` in `apps/web/src/sales-ops/leads/` replaces `LeadDialog` and the contact dialog: name and contact required; empresa picker creates a cliente inline in BOTH editions (`onCreateClient` seam, copy `Buscar ou criar novo cliente`); choosing an empresa prefills the contact (`sales_ops_clients.contact`) and the responsible seller when the cliente has one; dynamic fields rendered by kind from `GET /field-definitions`; the Cliente field cannot be removed from the form.
- Lead summary page route: `operacional/leads/:leadId` and `meus-dados/leads/:leadId` (the third segment of the existing pattern; today `leads` uses it for a stage id: the planner must move the stage-in-URL to a query string or keep both unambiguous and say how); clicking a card or a Lista row opens the SUMMARY, never the form; the summary has `Editar` (opens `LeadForm`), `Avançar` (a dialog listing only `nextStages`, asking próximo passo + data unless the destination is an exit), notes, and the timeline.
- Follow-ups list: a new view (for example `operacional/follow-ups` and `meus-dados/follow-ups`) with Minha/Equipe and the period filter.
- Kanban: a pipeline selector (component state or query string, not a new path segment), columns from `GET /pipelines`, colours and behaviour keyed on `type`/`exitKind` (no `kind ===` inline, the write-surface guard stays green), the period filter, and the existing drag still going through `MoveLeadDialog` as the one emitter.
- Proposta versus Vendido decided by stage type (`orcamento` versus exit `ganho`) on the lead side and by `status` on the proposta side; each Propostas submenu entry gets its own route under the existing pattern.
- Every list (Propostas, Prospecção Lista and Quadro, Follow-ups, Clientes where it has dates) has the period filter, São Paulo civil days.

import type { SalesEdition } from '@fxl-sales/shared-utils/sales-edition';
import { and, asc, eq, isNull, or, sql, type SQL } from 'drizzle-orm';
import type { getDb } from '../../../db/client.js';

export { LeadInputError };

/**
 * The lead entity's server half.
 *
 * Three things this file deliberately does NOT do, each of them a rule rather
 * than an omission:
 *
 *  - It writes NO ledger entry, for any operation, movement included. The ledger
 *    is hash-chained, queues behind a global tail lock and is never purged; a
 *    card move is high-frequency noise. `lead-contract.test.ts` reads these
 *    bytes and fails if the writer is ever imported. Deleting and restoring a
 *    lead DO write one, which is exactly why they live in `lead-trash-service.ts`
 *    and not here; this file only exports the board plumbing they share (the
 *    lock, the renumber, the identity predicate, the view).
 *  - It never inserts a cliente and never inserts a venda. Creating a lead is
 *    not a commercial event: it consumes no proposta sequence, writes no
 *    `code_suffix` and moves no financial number. The cliente is resolved or
 *    created at CONVERSION time, by the proposta wizard, and not before.
 *  - It never resolves a vendedor through the deprecated boolean mirror column
 *    on a pessoa. `sales_ops_person_funcoes` against the `vendedor` system
 *    função is the one authority, and the source read in the contract test is
 *    what keeps the mirror out of this file entirely.
 */

type Db = ReturnType<typeof getDb>;

/** The caller, built at the route boundary from the VERIFIED context only. */
export type LeadScope = {
  userId: string;
  email: string | null;
  isAdmin: boolean;
  /** The verified token display name, or null. Read only by the leads-edition auto-provision. */
  name?: string | null;
  /** Whether the verified `userRoles` carry `seller`. Read only by the leads-edition auto-provision. */
  hasSellerRole?: boolean;
  /**
   * The edition the auth middleware resolved from the verified token. Absent
   * means 'full', so every caller that does not pass it (the import executor,
   * the FXL routes before this field existed) keeps today's behaviour.
   */
  edition?: SalesEdition;
};

export type LeadView = {
  id: string;
  stageId: string;
  stageChangedAt: string;
  position: number;
  contactName: string;
  clientId: string | null;
  clientNameSnapshot: string;
  estimatedValueBrl: number;
  description: string | null;
  /** Leads-edition contact data (edicao-leads). Always present; null for FXL leads. */
  contactPhone: string | null;
  contactEmail: string | null;
  /** ISO civil day `YYYY-MM-DD`; never formatted through Date. */
  contactBirthDate: string | null;
  sellerPersonId: string | null;
  sellerNameSnapshot: string;
  saleId: string | null;
  saleStatus: string | null;
  saleCode: string | null;
  lostReason: string | null;
  products: Array<{ productId: string | null; productNameSnapshot: string }>;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string | null;
  // Phase 2 additive fields (unified CRM). `description`, `contactBirthDate` and `products` above
  // are PROJECTED from the descricao, nascimento and produtos field values.
  pipelineId: string;
  stageType: string;
  stageExitKind: string | null;
  fields: LeadFieldValue[];
};

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Caller identity and the seller predicate
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * Which pessoa the caller IS, inside the caller's own tenant transaction.
 *
 * Step 2 is a SELF-claim and only a self-claim, and it is not the third-party
 * person matching this repo rejects for the ledger. That rejected heuristic
 * guesses a THIRD PARTY's identity from unverified text, and getting it wrong
 * misattributes an entry forever. Here: the e-mail comes from the caller's own
 * VERIFIED Hub token, the row claimed is the caller's OWN, EXACTLY ONE candidate
 * is required, and the `hub_account_id IS NULL` predicate plus the partial
 * unique index make a second claim impossible. Four guards, all of them needed.
 *
 * The explicit admin repair path is `PATCH /people/:id {hubAccountId}`. Both
 * exist; neither is a fallback for the other.
 */
export async function resolveCallerPersonId(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<string | null> {
  const [bound] = await tx
    .select({ id: salesOpsPeople.id })
    .from(salesOpsPeople)
    .where(and(eq(salesOpsPeople.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsPeople.hubAccountId, scope.userId)))
    .limit(1);
  if (bound) return bound.id;

  const email = scope.email?.trim().toLowerCase() ?? '';
  if (email === '') return null;

  // TWO rows is deliberately a refusal and not a pick: two pessoas sharing one
  // e-mail is a cadastro the server cannot disambiguate, and choosing one would
  // silently scope a seller to somebody else's board.
  const candidates = await tx
    .select({ id: salesOpsPeople.id })
    .from(salesOpsPeople)
    .where(
      and(
        eq(salesOpsPeople.orgId, orgId),
        eq(salesOpsPeople.status, 'active'),
        sql`${salesOpsPeople.hubAccountId} is null`,
        sql`lower(btrim(${salesOpsPeople.contactEmail})) = ${email}`,
      ),
    )
    .limit(2);
  if (candidates.length !== 1) return null;

  const [claimed] = await tx
    .update(salesOpsPeople)
    .set({ hubAccountId: scope.userId, updatedAt: new Date() })
    .where(
      and(
        eq(salesOpsPeople.orgId, orgId),
        eq(salesOpsPeople.id, candidates[0]!.id),
        sql`${salesOpsPeople.hubAccountId} is null`,
      ),
    )
    .returning({ id: salesOpsPeople.id });
  if (claimed) return claimed.id;

  // A concurrent claim won. Re-read rather than guess.
  const [reread] = await tx
    .select({ id: salesOpsPeople.id })
    .from(salesOpsPeople)
    .where(and(eq(salesOpsPeople.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsPeople.hubAccountId, scope.userId)))
    .limit(1);
  return reread?.id ?? null;
}

export type LeadScopeGate =
  | {
      ok: true;
      /** The caller's own pessoa, or null for an admin (no seller predicate at all). */
      sellerPersonId: string | null;
      /**
       * Whether a non-admin caller sees, and claims on their first write, the
       * unassigned pool (leads-sem-vendedor). True only when the caller's pessoa
       * is an ACTIVE vendedor; always false for an admin, who never claims.
       */
      canClaimUnassigned: boolean;
    }
  | { ok: false; reason: 'seller_person_unmapped' };

/** The gate after its `ok` check: what the predicate and the claim are built from. */
export type LeadScopeAllowed = Extract<LeadScopeGate, { ok: true }>;

/**
 * The seller predicate, resolved on the SERVER and never derived from anything
 * the request body carries.
 *
 * It FAILS CLOSED. An unresolvable caller must never degrade into "no
 * predicate": that single mutation turns this whole slice into a cross-seller
 * data leak. The discriminated union is what makes it impossible to spell,
 * because a bare `string | null` would make `null` mean both "no predicate,
 * admin" and "no predicate, could not tell".
 *
 * A non-admin also carries `canClaimUnassigned`, true only for an active
 * vendedor: it widens the predicate to the unassigned pool and makes the first
 * write a claim (see `leadSellerCondition` and `claimantFor`).
 */
export async function resolveLeadScopePredicate(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<LeadScopeGate> {
  if (scope.isAdmin) return { ok: true, sellerPersonId: null, canClaimUnassigned: false };
  const personId = await resolveCallerPersonId(tx, orgId, scope);
  if (personId) return sellerGate(tx, orgId, personId);
  // The full edition never reaches the provisioning code: an FXL seller without
  // a pessoa is an operator cadastro gap and keeps answering unmapped.
  if (scope.edition === 'leads') {
    const provisioned = await provisionLeadsSellerPerson(tx, orgId, scope);
    if (provisioned) return sellerGate(tx, orgId, provisioned);
  }
  return { ok: false, reason: 'seller_person_unmapped' };
}

/**
 * A resolved non-admin caller. Resolving the pessoa says WHO the caller is;
 * whether they may see the unassigned pool is a separate question with a
 * separate answer, because a finder-only pessoa or a deactivated vendedor still
 * reaches their own leads (exactly as before this rule) but must never pick up a
 * lead from the pool.
 */
async function sellerGate(tx: Db, orgId: string, personId: string): Promise<LeadScopeGate> {
  return {
    ok: true,
    sellerPersonId: personId,
    canClaimUnassigned: (await findActiveVendedor(tx, orgId, personId)) !== null,
  };
}

/** `sales_ops_people.display_name` is capped by `PersonSchema` at this length. */
const PROVISIONED_NAME_MAX = 120;

/** The verified name, trimmed and capped, else the e-mail local part. */

/** The verified name, trimmed and capped, else the e-mail local part. */
function provisionedDisplayName(scope: LeadScope, email: string): string {
  let name = (scope.name ?? '').trim().slice(0, PROVISIONED_NAME_MAX);
  // Never cut a surrogate pair in half.
  if (/[\uD800-\uDBFF]$/.test(name)) name = name.slice(0, -1);
  name = name.trimEnd();
  if (name !== '') return name;
  const localPart = email.split('@')[0] ?? '';
  return localPart !== '' ? localPart : email;
}

const HUB_ACCOUNT_UNIQUE_INDEX = 'sales_ops_people_org_hub_account_idx';

/** A UNIQUE violation of the one-pessoa-per-Hub-account index (a concurrent provision or bind won). */
function isHubAccountUniqueViolation(error: unknown): boolean {
  // postgres.js puts code/constraint_name on the error; drizzle re-throws it
  // wrapped, exposing the original under `cause`.
  const candidates = [error, (error as { cause?: unknown } | null)?.cause];
  for (const candidate of candidates) {
    const pgError = candidate as
      | { code?: string; constraint_name?: string; constraint?: string }
      | null
      | undefined;
    if (!pgError || pgError.code !== '23505') continue;
    if ((pgError.constraint_name ?? pgError.constraint) === HUB_ACCOUNT_UNIQUE_INDEX) return true;
  }
  return false;
}

/**
 * Leads edition only: creates the caller's OWN pessoa on their first leads
 * request, after binding by account and the e-mail self-claim both found
 * nothing.
 *
 * Safe because the Hub already verified the account, the org and the app role
 * `seller`, and the row created is the caller's own, so scoping still shows
 * only their leads. It never guesses: any pessoa in the org already carrying
 * the token e-mail (two unbound ones, an inactive one, one bound elsewhere) is
 * a cadastro an admin made, and the caller stays `seller_person_unmapped`.
 *
 * Runs inside the request's tenant transaction through `createPersonTx` with
 * the leads edition, so the pessoa is exactly a vendedor and the system
 * funções are seeded in the same transaction.
 *
 * Concurrent first requests for one account. The transaction is READ
 * COMMITTED, so EVERY statement takes a fresh snapshot, and a winner may commit
 * between any two of the loser's statements. Two interleavings follow, and both
 * end on the winner's row through {@link boundPersonId}, re-read by account:
 *  - The winner commits after the loser's account lookup (a miss) but before
 *    its e-mail check, which then sees the winner's pessoa carrying the e-mail.
 *    Every e-mail hit re-reads by account BEFORE refusing, so a pessoa bound to
 *    this very account is returned as the caller's own.
 *  - The winner has not committed by the e-mail check. Both reach the INSERT;
 *    the partial unique index on (org_id, hub_account_id) makes the loser wait
 *    for the winner's commit and fail with 23505 inside a SAVEPOINT, so the
 *    request transaction survives, and the loser re-reads.
 * One account never gets two pessoas, and the loser is never refused.
 *
 * Exported for the oracle, which forces the first interleaving; the only
 * product caller is `resolveLeadScopePredicate`.
 */
export async function provisionLeadsSellerPerson(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<string | null> {
  if (scope.hasSellerRole !== true) return null;
  const email = scope.email?.trim().toLowerCase() ?? '';
  if (email === '') return null;

  const [existing] = await tx
    .select({ id: salesOpsPeople.id })
    .from(salesOpsPeople)
    .where(
      and(
        eq(salesOpsPeople.orgId, orgId),
        sql`lower(btrim(${salesOpsPeople.contactEmail})) = ${email}`,
      ),
    )
    .limit(1);
  // A fresh statement, so it sees a pessoa a concurrent request bound to this
  // account after our account lookup missed. Only a pessoa bound elsewhere, or
  // unbound, is somebody else's cadastro and a refusal.
  if (existing) return boundPersonId(tx, orgId, scope.userId);

  const input = PersonSchema.safeParse({
    displayName: provisionedDisplayName(scope, email),
    contactEmail: email,
    status: 'active',
    hubAccountId: scope.userId,
  });
  if (!input.success) return null;

  try {
    const created = await tx.transaction((savepoint) =>
      createPersonTx(savepoint as unknown as Db, orgId, input.data, { edition: 'leads' }),
    );
    return typeof created === 'string' ? null : created.id;
  } catch (error) {
    if (!isHubAccountUniqueViolation(error)) throw error;
  }
  // The 23505 is raised only after the winner committed, so this statement's
  // snapshot always contains the winner's row.
  return boundPersonId(tx, orgId, scope.userId);
}

/** The pessoa bound to the account, read with a fresh statement snapshot. */
async function boundPersonId(tx: Db, orgId: string, userId: string): Promise<string | null> {
  const [bound] = await tx
    .select({ id: salesOpsPeople.id })
    .from(salesOpsPeople)
    .where(and(eq(salesOpsPeople.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsPeople.hubAccountId, userId)))
    .limit(1);
  return bound?.id ?? null;
}

export type ResolvedSeller = { id: string; displayName: string };

/**
 * The pessoa when it is an ACTIVE vendedor, else null. The one spelling of that
 * rule in this file: the gate reads it to decide who sees the unassigned pool,
 * and `resolveSellerPersonId` reads it to decide who may be written as a lead's
 * vendedor, so the two can never disagree about who is a vendedor.
 */
async function findActiveVendedor(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  personId: string,
): Promise<ResolvedSeller | null> {
  const [seller] = await tx
    .select({ id: salesOpsPeople.id, displayName: salesOpsPeople.displayName })
    .from(salesOpsPeople)
    .innerJoin(
      salesOpsPersonFuncoes,
      and(
        eq(salesOpsPersonFuncoes.orgId, salesOpsPeople.orgId),
        eq(salesOpsPersonFuncoes.personId, salesOpsPeople.id),
      ),
    )
    .innerJoin(
      salesOpsFuncoes,
      and(
        eq(salesOpsFuncoes.orgId, salesOpsPersonFuncoes.orgId),
        eq(salesOpsFuncoes.id, salesOpsPersonFuncoes.funcaoId),
      ),
    )
    .where(
      and(
        eq(salesOpsPeople.orgId, orgId),
        eq(salesOpsPeople.id, personId),
        eq(salesOpsPeople.status, 'active'),
        eq(salesOpsFuncoes.slug, 'vendedor'),
        eq(salesOpsFuncoes.isSystem, true),
      ),
    )
    .limit(1);
  return seller ?? null;
}

/**
 * The vendedor, through `sales_ops_person_funcoes` against the `vendedor` SYSTEM
 * função. The returned display name is what gets written to
 * `seller_name_snapshot`: server-authoritative, exactly as `resolvePartyContexts`
 * makes `personNameSnapshot` server-authoritative, so a disagreeing body label
 * loses.
 */
export async function resolveSellerPersonId(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  sellerPersonId: string,
): Promise<ResolvedSeller> {
  const seller = await findActiveVendedor(tx, orgId, sellerPersonId);
  if (seller) return seller;

  // Tell the two apart, because they are two different operator mistakes: one is
  // a stale id, the other is a pessoa who simply does not sell.
  const [person] = await tx
    .select({ id: salesOpsPeople.id })
    .from(salesOpsPeople)
    .where(and(eq(salesOpsPeople.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsPeople.id, sellerPersonId)))
    .limit(1);
  throw new LeadInputError(person ? 'seller_not_a_vendedor' : 'seller_not_found');
}

/**
 * The empresa, probed with raw SQL rather than through the cadastro table object.
 *
 * The missing import is the point: a reader, and `lead-contract.test.ts`, can see
 * at a glance that this file has no way to CREATE a cliente. The returned name
 * becomes `client_name_snapshot` and a disagreeing body `clientName` loses.
 */
async function resolveClientName(tx: Db, orgId: string, clientId: string): Promise<string> {
  const rows = (await tx.execute(
    sql`SELECT name FROM sales_ops_clients WHERE org_id = ${orgId} AND id = ${clientId}::uuid LIMIT 1`,
  )) as unknown as Array<{ name: string }>;
  const name = rows[0]?.name;
  if (typeof name !== 'string') throw new LeadInputError('client_not_found');
  return name;
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Reads
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * The ONE spelling of "this lead is live" (lead-lixeira): deleted_at IS NULL.
 *
 * A deleted lead is a soft delete the gestor can restore, so its row stays and
 * every board read, count and write in this file steps around it through this
 * helper, directly or through `leadIdentityConditions`; so does the stage
 * summary. Only lead-trash-service.ts reads deleted rows, on purpose. A
 * hand-written deleted_at predicate anywhere else is a second rule that drifts.
 */
export function liveLeadCondition(): SQL {
  return isNull(salesOpsCrmLeads.deletedAt);
}

type LeadRow = typeof salesOpsCrmLeads.$inferSelect;

function toIso(value: Date | string | null): string | null {
  if (value === null) return null;
  return value instanceof Date ? value.toISOString() : new Date(value).toISOString();
}

/**
 * `sellerNameSnapshot` and `clientNameSnapshot` are PROJECTED from the row, not
 * re-joined: both columns are NOT NULL, this file is their only writer, and a
 * live join would be a second source of truth for the same label. Both are
 * rewritten on every write that could change them, which is what keeps them
 * fresh, and `sellerPersonId` travels beside the snapshot so the web can key on
 * the id without ever rendering it.
 *
 * `saleStatus` is read LIVE, because the converted column mirrors the proposta's
 * own status and nothing on the board may write it.
 *
 * Live only. Every caller has just proven the lead live inside this transaction,
 * or (a restore) just made it live, so a deleted row here is a bug and throws.
 */
export async function readLeadView(tx: Db, orgId: string, leadId: string): Promise<LeadView> {
  const [row] = await tx
    .select({ lead: salesOpsCrmLeads, stage: salesOpsPipelineStages, saleStatus: salesOpsSales.status, saleCode: salesOpsSales.code })
    .from(salesOpsCrmLeads)
    .innerJoin(salesOpsPipelineStages, stageJoin())
    .leftJoin(
      salesOpsSales,
      and(eq(salesOpsSales.orgId, salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId), eq(salesOpsSales.id, salesOpsCrmLeads.saleId)),
    )
    .where(and(eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.id, leadId), liveLeadCondition()))
    .limit(1);
  if (!row) throw new Error(`lead ${leadId} disappeared inside its own transaction`);
  const fields = await readLeadFields(tx, orgId, [row.lead]);
  return toLeadView(row.lead, row.stage, row.saleStatus, row.saleCode, fields.get(leadId) ?? []);
}

/** The lead's stage row: the composite FK guarantees it exists in the same org and pipeline. */
function stageJoin(): SQL {
  return and(
    eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.orgId, salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId),
    eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.id, salesOpsCrmLeads.stageId),
  )!;
}

type StageRow = typeof salesOpsPipelineStages.$inferSelect;

function toLeadView(
  lead: LeadRow,
  stage: StageRow,
  saleStatus: string | null,
  saleCode: string | null,
  fields: LeadFieldValue[],
): LeadView {
  const legacy = projectLegacyFields(fields);
  return {
    id: lead.id,
    stageId: lead.stageId,
    stageChangedAt: toIso(lead.stageChangedAt)!,
    position: lead.position,
    contactName: lead.contactName,
    clientId: lead.clientId,
    clientNameSnapshot: lead.clientNameSnapshot,
    estimatedValueBrl: lead.estimatedValueBrl,
    description: legacy.description,
    contactPhone: lead.contactPhone,
    contactEmail: lead.contactEmail,
    contactBirthDate: legacy.contactBirthDate,
    sellerPersonId: lead.sellerPersonId,
    sellerNameSnapshot: lead.sellerNameSnapshot,
    saleId: lead.saleId,
    saleStatus: lead.saleId ? saleStatus : null,
    // Guarded by `lead.saleId` for the same reason `saleStatus` is: the LEFT JOIN
    // yields a row either way, and a code without a link is a label pointing at
    // nothing. The board renders this as the card's only proposta identity.
    saleCode: lead.saleId ? saleCode : null,
    lostReason: lead.lostReason,
    products: legacy.products,
    createdAt: toIso(lead.createdAt)!,
    updatedAt: toIso(lead.updatedAt),
    pipelineId: lead.pipelineId,
    stageType: stage.type,
    stageExitKind: stage.exitKind,
    fields,
