import { z } from 'zod';
import { isAfterTodayInSaoPaulo, isIsoDay } from '@fxl-sales/shared-utils/sao-paulo-day';

/**
 * The lead entity's wire contract. Imports only zod and the pure sao-paulo-day subpath - no database, no service -
 * which is what keeps `lead-contract.test.ts` a pure unit test.
 *
 * WHY `.strict()`, a deliberate deviation from the rest of this domain.
 *
 * Everything else here lets zod strip unknown keys, and CLAUDE.md records what
 * that costs: `PATCH /clients/:id {"status":"archived"}` answers 200 with an
 * unchanged row, "a silent no-op that reads as success". The identical trap on a
 * board is a `PATCH /leads/:id` carrying `stageId`, `position`, `saleId` or
 * `lostReason`: it would answer 200, the card would snap back on the next read,
 * and nothing would say why. `.strict()` makes each of those a loud 400 naming
 * the offending key.
 *
 * It is also the STRUCTURAL guarantee that `stage_id` has exactly one writer.
 * There is no code path from `PATCH /leads/:id` to the stage column, so "
 * stage_changed_at only moves when the stage moves" is not a runtime branch
 * anyone can later forget.
 */

const uuid = z.string().uuid();
/** Integer CENTS, like every other money value in this product. */
const money = z.number().int().nonnegative();

/**
 * The same two numbers as HISTORY_DEFAULT_LIMIT / HISTORY_MAX_LIMIT, for the
 * same reason: one screenful with room to spare, and a ceiling that bounds the
 * worst-case payload.
 */
/** Text caps of the lead write contract; the import workbook columns are tested against them. */
export const LEAD_CONTACT_NAME_MAX = 140;
export const LEAD_CLIENT_NAME_MAX = 200;
export const LEAD_DESCRIPTION_MAX = 4000;

export const LEADS_DEFAULT_LIMIT = 50;
export const LEADS_MAX_LIMIT = 200;

export const LeadProductSchema = z
  .object({
    productId: uuid.optional(),
    /** The description for a productId-less row. DISCARDED when productId resolves. */
    productName: z.string().trim().min(1).max(140).optional(),
  })
  .strict()
  .superRefine((row, ctx) => {
    if (row.productId || row.productName) return;
    ctx.addIssue({
      code: z.ZodIssueCode.custom,
      path: ['productName'],
      message: 'productName is required when productId is absent',
    });
  });

/**
 * Optional text normalization shared by every nullable lead key: trimmed, and an empty string is
 * stored as NULL ("cleared"), while an ABSENT key stays absent (PATCH semantics).
 */
function blankToNull(value: string | null | undefined): string | null | undefined {
  if (value === undefined || value === null) return value;
  return value === '' ? null : value;
}

const ContactPhoneSchema = z.string().trim().max(40).nullish().transform(blankToNull);

const ContactEmailSchema = z
  .string()
  .trim()
  .toLowerCase()
  .max(254)
  .refine((value) => value === '' || z.string().email().safeParse(value).success, {
    message: 'contactEmail must be a valid e-mail address',
  })
  .nullish()
  .transform(blankToNull);

/**
 * A civil day, never after today in Sao Paulo. Compared as a string, never through Date.
 * Zod runs every refine even after an earlier one failed, so the future check
 * re-guards with `isIsoDay` (an impossible day is already reported above) instead
 * of letting `isAfterTodayInSaoPaulo` throw its RangeError.
 */
const ContactBirthDateSchema = z
  .string()
  .trim()
  .refine((value) => value === '' || isIsoDay(value), {
    message: 'contactBirthDate must be a real calendar day (YYYY-MM-DD)',
  })
  .refine((value) => value === '' || !isIsoDay(value) || !isAfterTodayInSaoPaulo(value), {
    message: 'contactBirthDate cannot be in the future',
  })
  .nullish()
  .transform(blankToNull);


/** One entry of `fields`: a dynamic field value, addressed by its definition id or its key. */
const FieldItemSchema = z
  .object({
    id: uuid.nullish(),
    name: z.string().trim().max(200).optional(),
  })
  .strict();

export const LeadFieldWriteSchema = z
  .object({
    fieldId: uuid.optional(),
    key: z.string().trim().min(1).max(60).optional(),
    /** text, number, money (integer cents), date (civil day), option key, or reference items; null clears. */
    value: z.union([z.string().max(4000), z.number(), z.null(), z.array(FieldItemSchema).max(50)]),
  })
  .strict()
  .refine((entry) => entry.fieldId !== undefined || entry.key !== undefined, {
    message: 'fieldId or key is required',
    path: ['key'],
  });

/**
 * THE lead write contract (phase 2 cutover): one schema for both editions. `contactName` is the
 * only required key; everything else is optional. The old keys `description`, `contactBirthDate`
 * and `products` are still accepted and stored as the `descricao`, `nascimento` and `produtos`
 * field values, so the web of today keeps working. `.strict()` stays: an unknown key is a loud 400.
 *
 * `LeadWriteBaseSchema` is the shape; `LeadWriteSchema` (create) adds the contact rule - at least
 * one of `contactPhone` or `contactEmail` - and `LeadPatchSchema` (PATCH) is the same shape with
 * every key optional and no `pipelineId` / `stageId` (a lead changes stage only through the move
 * and advance routes, so `stage_changed_at` has exactly one writer).
 * The spreadsheet import writes through the BASE schema: a workbook row has no contact column.
 */
const leadWriteShape = {
  contactName: z.string().trim().min(1).max(LEAD_CONTACT_NAME_MAX),
  contactPhone: ContactPhoneSchema,
  contactEmail: ContactEmailSchema,
  /** Create only. Absent means the org's default pipeline. */
  pipelineId: uuid.optional(),
  /** Create only. Absent means the first active normal stage of the pipeline. */
  stageId: uuid.optional(),
  /**
   * NULLABLE: a lead may sit unassigned. An unassigned lead is visible to admins and to every
   * active vendedor, and the first vendedor to write it claims it (see `claimantFor`).
   */
  sellerPersonId: z.preprocess((value) => (value === '' ? null : value), uuid.nullish()),
  /** The empresa. `clientId` resolves the snapshot; otherwise `clientName` is the free text. */
  clientId: z.preprocess((value) => (value === '' ? null : value), uuid.nullish()),
  clientName: z.string().trim().max(LEAD_CLIENT_NAME_MAX).nullish().transform(blankToNull),
  /** Integer CENTS. */
  estimatedValueBrl: money.default(0),
  fields: z.array(LeadFieldWriteSchema).max(50).optional(),
  // Legacy keys, mapped onto the descricao / nascimento / produtos field values.
  description: z.string().trim().max(LEAD_DESCRIPTION_MAX).nullish().transform(blankToNull),
  contactBirthDate: ContactBirthDateSchema,
  products: z.array(LeadProductSchema).max(50).optional(),
};

export const LeadWriteBaseSchema = z.object(leadWriteShape).strict();

export const LeadWriteSchema = LeadWriteBaseSchema.superRefine((lead, ctx) => {
  if (lead.contactPhone || lead.contactEmail) return;
  ctx.addIssue({
    code: z.ZodIssueCode.custom,
    path: ['contactPhone'],
    message: 'contactPhone or contactEmail is required',
  });
});

export const LeadPatchSchema = z
  .object(leadWriteShape)
  .omit({ pipelineId: true, stageId: true })
  .partial()
  .strict();

/**
 * The move wire body, and the field NAMES are the contract: slice 04's client
 * translates its own `toStageId` / `toIndex` into these exactly once. Renaming
 * one here silently breaks the board.
 */
export const MoveLeadSchema = z
  .object({
    stageId: uuid,
    /** 0-based target INDEX in the destination column, clamped to its length. */
    position: z.number().int().min(0).max(100_000),
    reason: z.string().trim().min(1).max(500).optional(),
    saleId: uuid.optional(),
  })
  .strict();

/**
 * The keyset cursor is the literal `(position, id)` pair the list orders by.
 * Validated by regex at the boundary so a malformed one is a 400 and never a
 * silently dropped filter.
 */
const CURSOR_RE =
  /^\d{1,9}:[0-9a-fA-F]{8}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{12}$/;

/**
 * `stageId` is REQUIRED. A kanban board loads one request per column, which is
 * how a column actually scrolls: each paginates independently, "carregar mais"
 * is per-column, and the cursor stays the simple pair above instead of a
 * three-part composite. It also caps the worst case at one column rather than
 * the whole board.
 */
const isoDay = z.string().refine((value) => isIsoDay(value), { message: 'must be a real calendar day (YYYY-MM-DD)' });

/** `from` / `to`: inclusive civil days in America/Sao_Paulo; a reversed range is a 400. */
const periodShape = {
  pipelineId: uuid.optional(),
  from: isoDay.optional(),
  to: isoDay.optional(),
};
const periodRefine = (query: { from?: string | undefined; to?: string | undefined }) =>
  query.from === undefined || query.to === undefined || query.from <= query.to;
const periodIssue = { message: 'from must not be after to', path: ['from'] };

export const ListLeadsQuerySchema = z
  .object({
    stageId: uuid,
    limit: z.coerce.number().int().min(1).max(LEADS_MAX_LIMIT).optional(),
    cursor: z.string().regex(CURSOR_RE).optional(),
    sellerPersonId: uuid.optional(),
    ...periodShape,
  })
  .refine(periodRefine, periodIssue);

/**
 * GET /leads/summary. No stageId: the summary covers every column of the pipeline in one GROUP BY.
 * The same narrowing keys as the list (seller, pipeline, period), so a column's total and the
 * summary's count are computed over the same set.
 */
export const LeadStageSummaryQuerySchema = z
  .object({ sellerPersonId: uuid.optional(), ...periodShape })
  .refine(periodRefine, periodIssue);

/** One dynamic filter parsed from `f.<fieldKey>[.min|.max]=<value>` (validated against the definitions in the service). */
export type DynamicFilterInput = { key: string; op: 'eq' | 'min' | 'max'; raw: string };

export type ListLeadsQuery = z.infer<typeof ListLeadsQuerySchema> & { filters?: DynamicFilterInput[] };
export type LeadStageSummaryQuery = z.infer<typeof LeadStageSummaryQuerySchema> & { filters?: DynamicFilterInput[] };

const blankToUndefined = (value: unknown) => (typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() === '' ? undefined : value);

/** POST /leads/:id/advance. Blank strings are read as absent (the web drops empties anyway). */
export const AdvanceLeadSchema = z
  .object({
    toStageId: uuid,
    nextStep: z.preprocess(blankToUndefined, z.string().trim().min(1).max(300).optional()),
    dueOn: z.preprocess(blankToUndefined, isoDay.optional()),
    note: z.preprocess(blankToUndefined, z.string().trim().min(1).max(4000).optional()),
    reason: z.preprocess(blankToUndefined, z.string().trim().min(1).max(500).optional()),
  })
  .strict();
export type AdvanceLeadInput = z.infer<typeof AdvanceLeadSchema>;

export const LeadNoteSchema = z.object({ body: z.string().trim().min(1).max(4000) }).strict();
export type LeadNoteInput = z.infer<typeof LeadNoteSchema>;

export const LeadFollowupSchema = z
  .object({
    nextStep: z.string().trim().min(1).max(300),
    dueOn: isoDay,
    ownerPersonId: z.preprocess((value) => (value === '' ? null : value), uuid.nullish()),
  })
  .strict();
export type LeadFollowupInput = z.infer<typeof LeadFollowupSchema>;

/** GET /followups. `mine` is the caller's; `team` needs an admin (checked at the route). */
export const ListFollowupsQuerySchema = z
  .object({
    scope: z.enum(['mine', 'team']).default('mine'),
    status: z.enum(['open', 'done', 'all']).default('open'),
    from: isoDay.optional(),
    to: isoDay.optional(),
    limit: z.coerce.number().int().min(1).max(LEADS_MAX_LIMIT).default(LEADS_MAX_LIMIT),
  })
  .refine(periodRefine, periodIssue);
export type ListFollowupsQuery = z.infer<typeof ListFollowupsQuerySchema>;

export type LeadWriteInput = z.infer<typeof LeadWriteBaseSchema>;
export type LeadPatchInput = z.infer<typeof LeadPatchSchema>;
export type MoveLeadInput = z.infer<typeof MoveLeadSchema>;

/**
 * The deleted-list keyset cursor: `<deleted_at as UTC ISO with MICROseconds>_<id>`.
 *
 * Microseconds and not the milliseconds a JS Date carries, because timestamptz
 * stores microseconds: a millisecond cursor would sit between two rows deleted
 * in the same millisecond and silently skip the later one. The server renders
 * it with to_char and parses it back with ::timestamptz, so it round-trips
 * exactly. The day goes through isIsoDay so an impossible date (2026-02-30) is
 * a 400 here and never a Postgres cast error (a 500).
 */
const DELETED_CURSOR_RE =
  /^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T([01]\d|2[0-3]):[0-5]\d:[0-5]\d\.\d{6}Z_[0-9a-fA-F]{8}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{12}$/;

export const ListDeletedLeadsQuerySchema = z.object({
  limit: z.coerce.number().int().min(1).max(LEADS_MAX_LIMIT).optional(),
  cursor: z
    .string()
    .regex(DELETED_CURSOR_RE)
    .refine((value) => isIsoDay(value.slice(0, 10)), {
      message: 'cursor day must be a real calendar day',
    })
    .optional(),
});

export type ListDeletedLeadsQuery = z.infer<typeof ListDeletedLeadsQuerySchema>;
export type LeadProductInput = z.infer<typeof LeadProductSchema>;


 succeeded in 0ms:

/**
 * The position that appends a card at the END of a column: one past the highest
 * LIVE position. A deleted lead keeps its stale position, and counting it would
 * leave a gap the moment the card above it is appended. The caller holds
 * `lockLeadBoard`, which is what makes two appends unable to share a number.
 */
export async function nextLeadPosition(tx: Db, orgId: string, stageId: string): Promise<number> {
  const [{ next }] = (await tx
    .select({ next: sql<number>`COALESCE(MAX(${salesOpsCrmLeads.position}), 0) + 1` })
    .from(salesOpsCrmLeads)
    .where(
      and(eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.stageId, stageId), liveLeadCondition()),
    )) as [{ next: number }];
  return next;
}

/** Resolves where a new lead lands: an explicit open stage of the pipeline, else the pipeline's first open one. */
async function resolveCreateStage(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  input: Pick<LeadWriteInput, 'pipelineId' | 'stageId'>,
): Promise<{ id: string; pipelineId: string }> {
  let pipelineId: string | null;
  if (input.pipelineId !== undefined) {
    if (!(await pipelineExists(tx, orgId, input.pipelineId))) throw new LeadInputError('pipeline_not_found');
    pipelineId = input.pipelineId;
  } else {
    pipelineId = await defaultPipelineId(tx, orgId);
  }
  // The edition-independent "no etapa yet" answer: an org with no pipeline, or a pipeline with no
  // open etapa, cannot take a new lead.
  if (pipelineId === null) throw new LeadInputError('no_open_stage');

  if (input.stageId !== undefined) {
    const [stage] = await tx
      .select({ id: salesOpsPipelineStages.id, type: salesOpsPipelineStages.type })
      .from(salesOpsPipelineStages)
      .where(
        and(
          eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.orgId, orgId),
          eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.pipelineId, pipelineId),
          eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.id, input.stageId),
          eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.status, 'active'),
        ),
      )
      .limit(1);
    if (!stage) throw new LeadInputError('stage_not_found');
    // A card that is converted, won or lost the instant it is born is not expressible.
    if (stage.type !== 'normal') throw new LeadInputError('stage_not_open');
    return { id: stage.id, pipelineId };
  }
  const first = await firstOpenLeadStage(tx, orgId, pipelineId);
  if (!first) throw new LeadInputError('no_open_stage');
  return { id: first.id, pipelineId };
}

async function insertLead(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  input: LeadWriteInput,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<WriteLeadResult> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    const gate = await resolveLeadScopePredicate(tx, orgId, scope);
    if (!gate.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gate.reason } as const;

    // Before MAX(position) below: a second create must wait for this one's row.
    await lockLeadBoard(tx, orgId);

    // A seller may only file their OWN leads, and may not file an unassigned one
    // either - `null !== gate.sellerPersonId` catches that. A loud 403 rather
    // than a 404, because they named the id themselves, so it leaks nothing.
    // An unnamed vendedor defaults to the caller (both editions), so only an
    // explicit OTHER id is refused.
    const named = input.sellerPersonId ?? null;
    const requestedSeller = gate.sellerPersonId && named === null ? gate.sellerPersonId : named;
    if (gate.sellerPersonId && requestedSeller !== gate.sellerPersonId) {
      return { ok: false, reason: 'seller_scope' } as const;
    }

    const seller = requestedSeller ? await resolveSellerPersonId(tx, orgId, requestedSeller) : null;

    const dynamic = await resolveFieldWrites(tx, orgId, input.fields ?? []);
    const legacy = await resolveLegacyFieldWrites(tx, orgId, input);
    const clientId = pickClientId(input.clientId, dynamic.cliente);
    const clientName = clientId
      ? await resolveClientName(tx, orgId, clientId)
      : (input.clientName ?? '');

    const stage = await resolveCreateStage(tx, orgId, input);
    const next = await nextLeadPosition(tx, orgId, stage.id);

    const [lead] = await tx
      .insert(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      .values({
        orgId,
        pipelineId: stage.pipelineId,
        stageId: stage.id,
        position: next,
        contactName: input.contactName,
        contactPhone: input.contactPhone ?? null,
        contactEmail: input.contactEmail ?? null,
        clientId,
        clientNameSnapshot: clientName,
        estimatedValueBrl: input.estimatedValueBrl ?? 0,
        sellerPersonId: seller?.id ?? null,
        sellerNameSnapshot: seller?.displayName ?? '',
        // Both literal, and both unreachable from LeadWriteSchema: a lead
        // cannot be born converted or born lost.
        saleId: null,
        lostReason: null,
        // stage_changed_at takes its column default, so "days parked" starts at
        // zero with no app-clock write.
      })
      .returning();

    // The one initial history entry of the lead (exactly one per lead, pinned by the partial unique
    // index): from NULL to its first stage, written by the server in the same transaction.
    await tx.insert(salesOpsCrmLeadEvents).values({
      orgId,
      leadId: lead!.id,
      kind: 'stage_changed',
      fromStageId: null,
      toStageId: stage.id,
      reason: null,
      actorUserId: scope.userId,
      actorNameSnapshot: scopeActorName(scope),
    });

    await applyFieldWrites(tx, orgId, lead!.id, [...legacy, ...dynamic.writes]);
    return { ok: true, lead: await readLeadView(tx, orgId, lead!.id) } as const;
  });
}

/**
 * The empresa of a write: the top-level `clientId` wins when the body carries it, the Cliente
 * field entry of `fields` applies otherwise. Naming both is ambiguous and refused.
 */
function pickClientId(
  topLevel: string | null | undefined,
  cliente: { clientId: string | null; index: number } | null,
): string | null {
  if (topLevel !== undefined && cliente !== null) throw new LeadInputError('field_invalid', cliente.index);
  if (topLevel !== undefined) return topLevel;
  return cliente?.clientId ?? null;
}

export function updateLead(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  id: string,
  input: LeadPatchInput,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<WriteLeadResult> {
  return applyLeadUpdate(db, orgId, id, input, scope);
}

async function applyLeadUpdate(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  id: string,
  input: LeadPatchInput,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<WriteLeadResult> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    const gate = await resolveLeadScopePredicate(tx, orgId, scope);
    if (!gate.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gate.reason } as const;

    const [current] = await tx
      .select()
      .from(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      .where(and(...leadIdentityConditions(orgId, id, gate)))
      .limit(1)
      .for('update');
    if (!current) return { ok: false, reason: 'not_found' } as const;
    // A converted card is read-only: its data now lives on the proposta.
    if (current.saleId !== null) return { ok: false, reason: 'already_converted' } as const;

    const claimant = claimantFor(gate, current);
    // Whether this UPDATE writes the seller pair at all: a claim always does,
    // otherwise only a body that names the key.
    const writesSeller = claimant !== null || input.sellerPersonId !== undefined;

    let seller: ResolvedSeller | null = null;
    if (claimant !== null) {
      // On an unassigned lead the caller may leave the vendedor out, clear it or
      // name themselves, and all three mean "mine": the web form of a pool lead
      // carries an empty vendedor. Any other id would hand the lead to a
      // colleague, which only a gestor may do, so it is refused before anything
      // is written. The snapshot is the pessoa's own display name, read here.
      const requested = input.sellerPersonId ?? null;
      if (requested !== null && requested !== claimant) {
        return { ok: false, reason: 'seller_scope' } as const;
      }
      seller = await resolveSellerPersonId(tx, orgId, claimant);
    } else if (input.sellerPersonId !== undefined) {
      const requested = input.sellerPersonId ?? null;
      if (gate.sellerPersonId && requested !== gate.sellerPersonId) {
        return { ok: false, reason: 'seller_scope' } as const;
      }
      seller = requested ? await resolveSellerPersonId(tx, orgId, requested) : null;
    }

    // A write that touches the contact must leave at least one of phone and e-mail.
    if (input.contactPhone !== undefined || input.contactEmail !== undefined) {
      const phone = input.contactPhone !== undefined ? input.contactPhone : current.contactPhone;
      const email = input.contactEmail !== undefined ? input.contactEmail : current.contactEmail;
      if (!phone && !email) throw new LeadInputError('contact_required');
    }

    const dynamic = await resolveFieldWrites(tx, orgId, input.fields ?? []);
    const legacy = await resolveLegacyFieldWrites(tx, orgId, input);
    const clientTouched = input.clientId !== undefined || dynamic.cliente !== null;
    const nextClientId = clientTouched ? pickClientId(input.clientId, dynamic.cliente) : current.clientId;

    let clientNameSnapshot: string | undefined;
    if (clientTouched) {
      clientNameSnapshot = nextClientId
        ? await resolveClientName(tx, orgId, nextClientId)
        : // The column is NOT NULL, so clearing the LINK can never blank the
          // label: the body's text wins, and the stored snapshot is the floor.
          (input.clientName ?? current.clientNameSnapshot);
    } else if (input.clientName !== undefined) {
      // NOT NULL floor: a null clear keeps the stored snapshot, matching the
      // clientId branch above.
      clientNameSnapshot = input.clientName ?? current.clientNameSnapshot;
    }

    await tx
      .update(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      // Built from `input` alone, and `LeadPatchSchema` is `.strict()` and
      // declares no stageId, position, stageChangedAt, saleId or lostReason - so
      // none of those keys can appear here, ever.
      .set({
        ...(input.contactName !== undefined ? { contactName: input.contactName } : {}),
        ...(clientTouched ? { clientId: nextClientId } : {}),
        ...(clientNameSnapshot !== undefined ? { clientNameSnapshot } : {}),
        ...(input.estimatedValueBrl !== undefined
          ? { estimatedValueBrl: input.estimatedValueBrl }
          : {}),
        ...(input.contactPhone !== undefined ? { contactPhone: input.contactPhone } : {}),
        ...(input.contactEmail !== undefined ? { contactEmail: input.contactEmail } : {}),
        ...(writesSeller
          ? {
              sellerPersonId: seller?.id ?? null,
              sellerNameSnapshot: seller?.displayName ?? '',
            }
          : {}),
        updatedAt: new Date(),
      })
      // Live again here, not only in the identity read: belt and braces under READ COMMITTED, the row lock above already re-checked it.
      .where(and(eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.id, id), liveLeadCondition()));

    // A key absent from the body leaves its value rows alone; an empty value clears them. The same
    // distinction `updateProduct` draws for the produto's função costs.
    await applyFieldWrites(tx, orgId, id, [...legacy, ...dynamic.writes]);

    return { ok: true, lead: await readLeadView(tx, orgId, id) } as const;
  });
}

export type MoveLeadOptions = {
  /**
   * The spreadsheet import places a historical lead straight into its etapa; there is no path to
   * walk. Only the import executor passes it. Every other caller is held to the transition graph.
   */
  skipTransitionGraph?: boolean;
};

/**
 * The writer of `stage_id`, `position`, `stage_changed_at`, `lost_reason` and `sale_id` for the
 * board (`advanceLead` in lead-history-service.ts is the other one, sharing the lock order, the
 * transition rule and the history event).
 */
export async function moveLead(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  id: string,
  input: MoveLeadInput,
  scope: LeadScope,
  options: MoveLeadOptions = {},
): Promise<WriteLeadResult> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    const gate = await resolveLeadScopePredicate(tx, orgId, scope);
    if (!gate.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gate.reason } as const;
    // Before the card's row lock, never after it: see lockLeadBoard.
    await lockLeadBoard(tx, orgId);

    const [current] = await tx
      .select()
      .from(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      .where(and(...leadIdentityConditions(orgId, id, gate)))
      .limit(1)
      .for('update');
    if (!current) return { ok: false, reason: 'not_found' } as const;

    // The read-only final column, enforced on the server and not merely
    // un-draggable in the UI. It is also what makes "no board action reaches
    // POST /sales/:id/transition" true by construction: once a lead carries a
    // proposta there is no lead operation left that could.
    if (current.saleId !== null) return { ok: false, reason: 'already_converted' } as const;

    // An ARCHIVED stage is not a move target, and neither is a stage of another pipeline (the
    // composite FK (org, pipeline, stage) would refuse it anyway).
    const [destination] = await tx
      .select({
        id: salesOpsPipelineStages.id,
        pipelineId: salesOpsPipelineStages.pipelineId,
        type: salesOpsPipelineStages.type,
        behavior: salesOpsPipelineStages.behavior,
        exitKind: salesOpsPipelineStages.exitKind,
        requiresReason: salesOpsPipelineStages.requiresReason,
      })
      .from(salesOpsPipelineStages)
      .where(
        and(
          eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.orgId, orgId),
          eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.id, input.stageId),
          eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.status, 'active'),
        ),
      )
      .limit(1);
    if (!destination) throw new LeadInputError('stage_not_found');

    const stageChanged = destination.id !== current.stageId;
    // A reorder inside one column is always allowed. A cross-stage move must be an edge of the
    // pipeline's explicit transition graph (a stage of another pipeline is never reachable).
    if (stageChanged && !options.skipTransitionGraph) {
      const allowed =
        destination.pipelineId === current.pipelineId &&
        (await stageTransitionAllowed(tx, orgId, current.pipelineId, current.stageId, destination.id));
      if (!allowed) return { ok: false, reason: 'transition_not_allowed' } as const;
    } else if (destination.pipelineId !== current.pipelineId) {
      // Only the import can get here (graph skipped); the composite FK would refuse it anyway.
      throw new LeadInputError('stage_not_found');
    }

    // This cannot be a zod refine: the requirement depends on the DESTINATION
    // row, which is a database read. So it is the service sentinel 400, the same
    // shape routes.ts already returns for entrada_mode_value_mismatch.
    if (destination.requiresReason && input.reason === undefined) {
      throw new LeadInputError('lost_reason_required');
    }

    const opensOrcamento = destination.type === 'orcamento' && destination.behavior === 'opens_orcamentos';
    if (opensOrcamento) {
      if (input.saleId === undefined) throw new LeadInputError('sale_required_for_conversion');
      // The in-org SELECT is what produces the designed 400. The composite FK
      // sales_ops_crm_leads_org_sale_fk is the backstop underneath it: without this
      // read a cross-org id would surface as a raw 23503 and an HTTP 500.
      const [sale] = await tx
        .select({ id: salesOpsSales.id })
        .from(salesOpsSales)
        .where(and(eq(salesOpsSales.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsSales.id, input.saleId)))
        .limit(1);
      if (!sale) throw new LeadInputError('sale_not_found');
    } else if (input.saleId !== undefined) {
      // sale_id and "is in the conversion column" can never diverge.
      throw new LeadInputError('sale_not_allowed');
    }

    // The claim (leads-sem-vendedor), resolved after every validation above so a
    // refused move never claims, and written by the same UPDATE as the move.
    const claimant = claimantFor(gate, current);
    const claimed = claimant !== null ? await resolveSellerPersonId(tx, orgId, claimant) : null;

    await tx
      .update(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      .set({
        stageId: destination.id,
        // KEY OMISSION, never `stageChanged ? now : current.stageChangedAt`. A
        // reorder inside one stage produces an UPDATE whose `set` object has no
        // stage_changed_at key at all, so the column is physically untouchable
        // by a reorder - the rule is not a branch someone can later "simplify".
        ...(stageChanged ? { stageChangedAt: new Date() } : {}),
        lostReason: destination.exitKind === 'perdido' ? (input.reason ?? null) : null,
        ...(opensOrcamento ? { saleId: input.saleId! } : {}),
        ...(claimed ? { sellerPersonId: claimed.id, sellerNameSnapshot: claimed.displayName } : {}),
        updatedAt: new Date(),
      })
      .where(and(eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.id, id), liveLeadCondition()));

    await renumberStage(tx, orgId, destination.id, { moved: id, at: input.position });
    if (stageChanged) {
      await renumberStage(tx, orgId, current.stageId, null);
      // Exactly one history event per stage change, written by the server in this transaction.
      await recordStageChange(tx, {
        orgId,
        leadId: id,
        fromStageId: current.stageId,
        toStageId: destination.id,
        reason: destination.exitKind === 'perdido' ? (input.reason ?? null) : null,
        actorUserId: scope.userId,
        actorName: scopeActorName(scope),
      });
    }

    return { ok: true, lead: await readLeadView(tx, orgId, id) } as const;
  });
}

export type ListLeadsResult =
  | { ok: true; leads: LeadView[]; nextCursor: string | null; total: number }
  | { ok: false; reason: 'seller_person_unmapped' };

/**
 * One column of the board, keyset-paginated.
 *
 * CURSOR and not OFFSET, copying `listOrgAuditHistory`'s shape so the repo keeps
 * one pagination idiom - and because it is the only correct choice here: a
 * kanban column is reordered constantly, and OFFSET over a set whose sort key is
 * being rewritten silently skips and duplicates cards between pages. The sort
 * key is the PAIR `(position, id)`: `position` is the order the operator set and
 * `id` is what makes the key total while a renumber is in flight.
 */
export async function listLeads(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  query: ListLeadsQuery,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<ListLeadsResult> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    const gate = await resolveLeadScopePredicate(tx, orgId, scope);
    if (!gate.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gate.reason } as const;

    // The count below and the page share this one `conditions` array, so `total`
    // counts exactly the rows a page can return.
    const conditions = leadBoardConditions(orgId, gate, query.sellerPersonId);
    conditions.push(eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.stageId, query.stageId));
    conditions.push(...(await boardNarrowingConditions(tx, orgId, query, { defaultToDefaultPipeline: false })));

    const [{ total }] = (await tx
      .select({ total: sql<number>`count(*)::int` })
      .from(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      .where(and(...conditions))) as [{ total: number }];

    if (query.cursor) {
      const [position, id] = query.cursor.split(':');
      conditions.push(
        sql`(${salesOpsCrmLeads.position}, ${salesOpsCrmLeads.id}) > (${Number(position)}, ${id}::uuid)`,
      );
    }

    const limit = query.limit ?? LEADS_DEFAULT_LIMIT;
    const rows = await tx
      .select({ lead: salesOpsCrmLeads, stage: salesOpsPipelineStages, saleStatus: salesOpsSales.status, saleCode: salesOpsSales.code })
      .from(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      .innerJoin(salesOpsPipelineStages, stageJoin())
      .leftJoin(
        salesOpsSales,
        and(
          eq(salesOpsSales.orgId, salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId),
          eq(salesOpsSales.id, salesOpsCrmLeads.saleId),
        ),
      )
      .where(and(...conditions))
      .orderBy(asc(salesOpsCrmLeads.position), asc(salesOpsCrmLeads.id))
      .limit(limit + 1);

    const hasMore = rows.length > limit;
    const page = hasMore ? rows.slice(0, limit) : rows;
    const fields = await readLeadFields(
      tx,
      orgId,
      page.map((row) => row.lead),
    );
    const last = page.at(-1);
    return {
      ok: true,
      leads: page.map((row) =>
        toLeadView(row.lead, row.stage, row.saleStatus, row.saleCode, fields.get(row.lead.id) ?? []),
      ),
      nextCursor: hasMore && last ? `${last.lead.position}:${last.lead.id}` : null,
      total,
    } as const;
  });
}

export type LeadStageTotals = { stageId: string; count: number; estimatedValueBrl: number };

export type LeadStageSummaryResult =
  | { ok: true; stages: LeadStageTotals[] }
  | { ok: false; reason: 'seller_person_unmapped' };

/**
 * The true per-column count and value for exactly the set the board may show,
 * in ONE grouped read: the board loads a column 50 cards at a time, so any total
 * computed from loaded cards undercounts a long column.
 *
 * Same gate and same WHERE as `listLeads` (`leadBoardConditions`), minus the
 * column: live leads only, converted and lost leads included because the board
 * lists them. Only stages with at least one visible lead appear; a missing stage
 * means zero. No stage join: the web keys each column by `stageId`.
 *
 * Money. `estimated_value_brl` is int4, NOT NULL, CHECK >= 0, so one lead is at
 * most 2_147_483_647 cents and `sum()` over int4 is int8 in Postgres. postgres.js
 * returns int8 as a string, hence `mapWith(Number)`. A stage total stays exact in
 * a JS number up to 2^53 - 1, which needs more than 4_194_304 leads in one stage
 * all at the int4 maximum; the guard below turns that unreachable case into a
 * loud error instead of a silently rounded total.
 */
export async function summarizeLeadStages(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  query: LeadStageSummaryQuery,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<LeadStageSummaryResult> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    const gate = await resolveLeadScopePredicate(tx, orgId, scope);
    if (!gate.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gate.reason } as const;

    const rows = await tx
      .select({
        stageId: salesOpsCrmLeads.stageId,
        count: sql<number>`count(*)::int`,
        estimatedValueBrl: sql<string>`sum(${salesOpsCrmLeads.estimatedValueBrl})::bigint`.mapWith(Number),
      })
      .from(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      .where(
        and(
          ...leadBoardConditions(orgId, gate, query.sellerPersonId),
          ...(await boardNarrowingConditions(tx, orgId, query, { defaultToDefaultPipeline: true })),
        ),
      )
      .groupBy(salesOpsCrmLeads.stageId)
      .orderBy(asc(salesOpsCrmLeads.stageId));

    return {
      ok: true,
      stages: rows.map((row) => {
        if (!Number.isSafeInteger(row.estimatedValueBrl)) {
          throw new Error(`lead stage ${row.stageId} value total exceeds the exact integer range`);
        }
        return { stageId: row.stageId, count: row.count, estimatedValueBrl: row.estimatedValueBrl };
      }),
    } as const;
  });
}

export type ReadLeadResult =
  | { ok: true; lead: LeadView }
  | { ok: false; reason: 'not_found' | 'seller_person_unmapped' };

/**
 * An out-of-scope lead reads as `not_found` and never as `forbidden`: a 403 on a
 * specific uuid confirms the row exists.
 */
export async function getLead(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  id: string,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<ReadLeadResult> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    const gate = await resolveLeadScopePredicate(tx, orgId, scope);
    if (!gate.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gate.reason } as const;
    const [row] = await tx
      .select({ id: salesOpsCrmLeads.id })
      .from(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      .where(and(...leadIdentityConditions(orgId, id, gate)))
      .limit(1);
    if (!row) return { ok: false, reason: 'not_found' } as const;
    return { ok: true, lead: await readLeadView(tx, orgId, id) } as const;
  });
}

/**
 * The seller predicate for a non-admin caller, and the ONE place it is spelled.
 *
 * An active vendedor sees their own leads plus the unassigned pool
 * (leads-sem-vendedor), written as the explicit disjunction
 * `seller = me OR seller IS NULL` and never as IS NOT DISTINCT FROM. `or()`
 * parenthesizes it, which is load-bearing: unparenthesized, the IS NULL arm
 * would escape the org conjunct beside it and reach every org's pool. Anyone
 * else keeps exactly their own leads.
 */
export function leadSellerCondition(sellerPersonId: string, canClaimUnassigned: boolean): SQL {
  const own = eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.sellerPersonId, sellerPersonId);
  if (!canClaimUnassigned) return own;
  return or(own, isNull(salesOpsCrmLeads.sellerPersonId))!;
}

/**
 * `eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId, orgId)` is ALWAYS the first element, and the seller
 * predicate is appended only for a non-admin. The admin case is the same
 * expression minus one conjunct. It always carries `liveLeadCondition()`, so a
 * deleted lead is `not_found` to every identity read (get, PATCH, move, delete)
 * for every caller, admin included. Exported for lead-trash-service.ts.
 */
export function leadIdentityConditions(orgId: string, id: string, gate: LeadScopeAllowed): SQL[] {
  const conditions: SQL[] = [
    eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId, orgId),
    eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.id, id),
    liveLeadCondition(),
  ];
  if (gate.sellerPersonId) {
    conditions.push(leadSellerCondition(gate.sellerPersonId, gate.canClaimUnassigned));
  }
  return conditions;
}

/**
 * The board read's WHERE minus the column: the org, live leads only, and the
 * seller rule. `listLeads` and `summarizeLeadStages` both start from it, so a
 * column's `total` and the summary's `count` can never be computed over two
 * different sets.
 *
 * The `else if` is the whole seller-scoping rule: for a non-admin the predicate
 * is built from the caller's OWN person id (plus the unassigned pool for an
 * active vendedor) and the requested `sellerPersonId` is never read AT ALL. A
 * seller who passes a colleague's id gets their own board back - not a 403, and
 * not the colleague's. For an admin it is an optional narrowing.
 */
function leadBoardConditions(
  orgId: string,
  gate: LeadScopeAllowed,
  requestedSellerPersonId: string | undefined,
): SQL[] {
  const conditions: SQL[] = [eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId, orgId), liveLeadCondition()];
  if (gate.sellerPersonId) {
    conditions.push(leadSellerCondition(gate.sellerPersonId, gate.canClaimUnassigned));
  } else if (requestedSellerPersonId) {
    conditions.push(eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.sellerPersonId, requestedSellerPersonId));
  }
  return conditions;
}

/**
 * The pessoa a write on `current` claims the lead for, or null for no claim.
 *
 * Only a non-admin caller who may see the pool claims (an admin never does: the
 * lead stays in the pool, gestor decision), and only a lead that was still
 * unassigned when its row lock was taken. Callers decide this AFTER the
 * `already_converted` refusal and the lead UPDATE carries it, so a refused write
 * never claims and a thrown one is rolled back with the transaction.
 *
 * The race guard is the `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` that produced `current`. A
 * second vendedor's PATCH on the same unassigned lead blocks on that row lock, and
 * READ COMMITTED re-checks its WHERE against the committed row once the first
 * commits. A second MOVE already waited on the board lock (`lockLeadBoard`)
 * before its identity read, so that read is a fresh statement that sees the
 * committed claim. Either way `seller = A` matches neither `seller = B` nor
 * `IS NULL`, so the second reads no row and answers not_found. Never raise these
 * transactions to REPEATABLE READ: the loser would then fail with 40001 (a 500)
 * instead.
 *
 * The `canClaimUnassigned` conjunct is redundant with the read predicate today
 * and kept so a future predicate change can never turn a finder into a claimant.
 */
export function claimantFor(gate: LeadScopeAllowed, current: LeadRow): string | null {
  if (gate.sellerPersonId === null || !gate.canClaimUnassigned) return null;
  return current.sellerPersonId === null ? gate.sellerPersonId : null;
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

export type WriteLeadResult =
  | { ok: true; lead: LeadView }
  | {
      ok: false;
      reason:
        | 'not_found'
        | 'seller_scope'
        | 'seller_person_unmapped'
        | 'already_converted'
        // Phase 2b: the destination is not reachable from the lead's stage in its pipeline graph
        | 'transition_not_allowed'
        // Phase 2b (advance only): the destination opens a proposta, which the wizard owns
        | 'sale_required_for_conversion';
    };

/** The snapshot name of the actor of a write: the verified token name, else the e-mail, else null. */
export function scopeActorName(scope: LeadScope): string | null {
  const name = scope.name?.trim();
  if (name) return name;
  const email = scope.email?.trim();
  return email ? email : null;
}

/**
 * The single create path (the full and the contact wire shapes collapsed into `LeadWriteSchema`).
 * In BOTH editions a non-admin seller who names no vendedor files the lead as their own; an
 * explicit OTHER id is still `403 seller_scope`.
 */
export function createLead(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  input: LeadWriteInput,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<WriteLeadResult> {
  return insertLead(db, orgId, input, scope);
}

/**
 * The org's lead board lock: the ONE place the lock order of a board write is
 * decided.
 *
 * Every transaction that writes a lead's `stage_id` or `position` (`moveLead`,
 * `insertLead`, and `deleteLead` / `restoreLead` in lead-trash-service.ts) takes it right after its scope gate and BEFORE it reads or locks
 * any lead row. Without it two writers sharing a column lock rows in opposite
 * orders: a move locks its own card first and then the whole destination and
 * source columns, so two vendedores dragging two cards out of one column each
 * held their own card while waiting for the other's, and Postgres aborted one
 * with 40P01, an HTTP 500. A create read MAX(position) with no lock at all, so
 * two creates could share a position. One lock per org, held to the end of the
 * writer's transaction, serializes them all. A holder may still wait on a card a
 * concurrent PATCH has locked, but a PATCH never asks for the board, so no
 * cycle can close.
 *
 * Why an advisory lock, and why per ORG:
 *  - Locking the two stage rows instead would collide with `reorderLeadStages`,
 *    which locks every active stage FOR UPDATE in position order, and with the
 *    foreign-key check of every lead INSERT. A key nothing else ever takes
 *    cannot.
 *  - Per stage would need the source stage before the card is read, a re-check
 *    after locking, and an out-of-order second lock whenever a concurrent move
 *    won in between. A board is one team dragging cards by hand: per org costs
 *    nothing measurable and leaves no order to get wrong.
 *
 * Transaction scoped (`_xact_`): COMMIT or ROLLBACK releases it and nothing else
 * can, so no path leaks it. Taken inside a SAVEPOINT (the import executor runs
 * these services on its own transaction) it passes to the parent when the
 * savepoint is released, so an import holds the board until it commits. The two
 * int4 keys are a namespace and the org; an org hash collision only makes two
 * boards share one queue. The key is the one the pre-cutover board used, unchanged.
 */
export async function lockLeadBoard(tx: Db, orgId: string): Promise<void> {
  await tx.execute(
    sql`SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('fxl-sales:lead-board'), hashtext(${orgId}))`,
  );
}

/**
 * The first ACTIVE normal stage of a pipeline by board order: where a new lead lands, and
 * where a restored lead lands when its own etapa was archived. One spelling of
 * the rule for both. `name` is the tiebreaker, matching the stage list's own
 * (position, name) read order. Without `pipelineId` it is the org's default pipeline. Null when
 * the pipeline has no open etapa.
 */
export async function firstOpenLeadStage(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  pipelineId?: string,
): Promise<{ id: string; name: string; pipelineId: string } | null> {
  const resolved = pipelineId ?? (await defaultPipelineId(tx, orgId));
  if (resolved === null) return null;
  const [stage] = await tx
    .select({
      id: salesOpsPipelineStages.id,
      name: salesOpsPipelineStages.name,
      pipelineId: salesOpsPipelineStages.pipelineId,
    })
    .from(salesOpsPipelineStages)
    .where(
      and(
        eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.orgId, orgId),
        eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.pipelineId, resolved),
        eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.status, 'active'),
        eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.type, 'normal'),
      ),
    )
    .orderBy(asc(salesOpsPipelineStages.position), asc(salesOpsPipelineStages.name))
    .limit(1);
  return stage ?? null;
}

/**
 * Writes dense 1..N over one column, in ONE statement.
 *
 * Every caller holds the board lock (`lockLeadBoard`), and THAT is what
 * serializes two drags: no other writer of a position can touch this column until
 * the caller's transaction ends, so the read below already sees every committed
 * move. The `FOR UPDATE` is no longer the guard. It stays because it is harmless:
 * it can only wait on a card a concurrent PATCH holds, and a PATCH never asks for
 * the board, so the wait cannot close a cycle.
 *
 * Over LIVE cards only. A deleted card keeps its stale position untouched (it is
 * irrelevant while deleted, and a restore appends at the end), and the raw UPDATE
 * below can only reach ids this live read returned.
 */
export async function renumberStage(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  stageId: string,
  placement: { moved: string; at: number } | null,
): Promise<void> {
  const rows = await tx
    .select({ id: salesOpsCrmLeads.id })
    .from(salesOpsCrmLeads)
    .where(
      and(
        eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId, orgId),
        eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.stageId, stageId),
        liveLeadCondition(),
      ),
    )
    .orderBy(asc(salesOpsCrmLeads.position), asc(salesOpsCrmLeads.id))
    .for('update');

  let ids = rows.map((row) => row.id);
  if (placement) {
    ids = ids.filter((id) => id !== placement.moved);
    // Clamped rather than refused: an out-of-range index parks the card at the
    // end of the column, which is what a drag past the last card means.
    ids.splice(Math.min(placement.at, ids.length), 0, placement.moved);
  }
  if (ids.length === 0) return;

  // Both casts are load-bearing: a bare VALUES row has no column types, so
  // Postgres infers text for each parameter and the comparison against the
  // integer column fails to resolve an operator.
  const values = ids.map((id, index) => sql`(${id}::uuid, ${index + 1}::int)`);
  await tx.execute(sql`
    UPDATE sales_ops_crm_leads AS l SET "position" = v.pos
    FROM (VALUES ${sql.join(values, sql`, `)}) AS v(id, pos)
    WHERE l.org_id = ${orgId} AND l.id = v.id AND l."position" IS DISTINCT FROM v.pos
  `);
}

import { planPipelineTransitions, type TransitionPlanStage } from '@fxl-sales/shared-utils/crm-model';
import { and, asc, eq, max, ne } from 'drizzle-orm';
import type { getDb } from '../../../db/client.js';
import { salesOpsPipelineStages, salesOpsPipelineTransitions } from '../../../db/schema.js';
import { defaultPipelineId, listPipelineStages, pipelineExists } from '../../crm/pipeline-read.js';
import { ensureBareDefaultPipeline } from '../../crm/pipeline-write.js';
import { legacyStageIsSystem, toLeadStageView, type LeadStageView } from '../../crm/stage-view.js';
import { ensureLeadStages } from './stages-seed.js';
import { withTenant } from './with-tenant.js';
import type { LeadStageInput } from './schemas.js';

type Db = ReturnType<typeof getDb>;

export type LeadStageRow = LeadStageView;

/**
 * Phase 2 cutover: these functions read and write the unified `sales_ops_pipeline_stages`. The
 * response keeps the old row shape (`kind`, `isSystem`, derived) and adds `pipelineId`, `type`,
 * `behavior`, `exitKind` and `requiresReason`. Without an explicit pipeline every function works
 * on the org's DEFAULT pipeline.
 *
 * There is exactly ONE duplicate sentinel on this surface.
 *
 * Unlike createFuncao there is no 'duplicate_slug' and no 'reserved_slug': 01
 * ships one uniqueness rule a caller can trip, sales_ops_lead_stages_org_name_idx
 * on (org_id, name), while `kind` carries the stage semantics and is unreachable
 * from the API. Do not re-add the funções' second member by symmetry.
 */
export type LeadStageDuplicate = 'duplicate';

/**
 * Maps a Postgres unique violation on sales_ops_lead_stages onto the service
 * sentinel, so a lost race reports the same reason the pre-check would have.
 * Same shape as mapFuncaoUniqueViolation, keyed on the ONE index name.
 *
 * The (org_id, kind) index is also unique, but nothing in this slice can write
 * `kind`, so it can never be the constraint a PATCH violates.
 */
const LEAD_STAGE_UNIQUE_VIOLATIONS: Record<string, LeadStageDuplicate> = {
  sales_ops_pipeline_stages_org_pipeline_name_idx: 'duplicate',
};

function mapLeadStageUniqueViolation(error: unknown): LeadStageDuplicate | null {
  // postgres.js puts code/constraint_name on the error; drizzle re-throws it
  // wrapped, exposing the original under `cause`.
  const candidates = [error, (error as { cause?: unknown } | null)?.cause];
  for (const candidate of candidates) {
    const pgError = candidate as
      | { code?: string; constraint_name?: string; constraint?: string }
      | null
      | undefined;
    if (!pgError || pgError.code !== '23505') continue;
    const constraint = pgError.constraint_name ?? pgError.constraint;
    const mapped = constraint ? LEAD_STAGE_UNIQUE_VIOLATIONS[constraint] : undefined;
    if (mapped) return mapped;
  }
  return null;
}

/** Per-pipeline uniqueness for the display name. One rule, so one probe. */
async function findLeadStageClash(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  pipelineId: string,
  name: string,
  excludeId?: string,
): Promise<LeadStageDuplicate | null> {
  const notSelf = excludeId ? [ne(salesOpsPipelineStages.id, excludeId)] : [];
  const [byName] = await tx
    .select({ id: salesOpsPipelineStages.id })
    .from(salesOpsPipelineStages)
    .where(
      and(
        eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.orgId, orgId),
        eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.pipelineId, pipelineId),
        eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.name, name),
        ...notSelf,
      ),
    )
    .limit(1);
  return byName ? 'duplicate' : null;
}

/** The pipeline a request addresses: the explicit one when it exists in the org, else the default. */
async function resolvePipeline(tx: Db, orgId: string, requested?: string): Promise<string | null> {
  if (requested !== undefined) return (await pipelineExists(tx, orgId, requested)) ? requested : null;
  return defaultPipelineId(tx, orgId);
}

/**
 * The pipeline's stages in board order.
 *
 * It returns ARCHIVED stages too, a deliberate divergence from the four audited
 * cadastros. A lead can still be sitting on a stage an admin just archived, and
 * a lead stage writes NO audit_log entry, so `Histórico de arquivamentos` is not
 * a restore surface for it: the `cadastros/etapas` screen is the only place a
 * stage can be restored from, and it can only offer that if this read returns
 * the archived row. The board filters to `status === 'active'` itself.
 *
 * Seeds the default CRM pipeline when the org has no pipeline row at all (ECV bug); see
 * ensureLeadStages. An unknown `pipelineId` answers an empty list.
 */
export function listLeadStages(db: Db, orgId: string, pipelineId?: string): Promise<LeadStageRow[]> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    const seeded = await ensureLeadStages(tx, orgId);
    if (pipelineId === undefined) return seeded;
    if (!(await pipelineExists(tx, orgId, pipelineId))) return [];
    return (await listPipelineStages(tx, orgId, pipelineId)).map(toLeadStageView);
  });
}

export type LeadStageCreateInput = LeadStageInput & { pipelineId?: string | undefined };

/** Creates a stage in the org's DEFAULT pipeline (the import executor and every pre-cutover caller). */
export async function createLeadStage(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  data: LeadStageInput,
): Promise<LeadStageRow | LeadStageDuplicate> {
  // Without a pipelineId the default pipeline is used, so 'pipeline_not_found' is unreachable.
  return (await createLeadStageInPipeline(db, orgId, data)) as LeadStageRow | LeadStageDuplicate;
}

export function createLeadStageInPipeline(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  data: LeadStageCreateInput,
): Promise<LeadStageRow | LeadStageDuplicate | 'pipeline_not_found'> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    // An org that never read its etapas has no pipeline yet: its first etapa creates the bare
    // default pipeline and the org is then never seeded (the pre-cutover "own first etapa" rule).
    const pipelineId =
      data.pipelineId === undefined
        ? await ensureBareDefaultPipeline(tx, orgId)
        : await resolvePipeline(tx, orgId, data.pipelineId);
    if (pipelineId === null) return 'pipeline_not_found' as const;

    // Fast path, purely for the error message: it names which rule was hit
    // without waiting on a lock. It is NOT the guard - see the conflict clause.
    const clash = await findLeadStageClash(tx, orgId, pipelineId, data.name);
    if (clash) return clash;

    // The max is taken over EVERY stage in the pipeline, archived included, so a new
    // stage can never land on an archived stage's position and a later restore
    // cannot collide. A count(*)-based position would.
    const [{ value: highest } = { value: null }] = await tx
      .select({ value: max(salesOpsPipelineStages.position) })
      .from(salesOpsPipelineStages)
      .where(and(eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.pipelineId, pipelineId)));
    const position = (highest ?? -1) + 1;

    const [stage] = await tx
      .insert(salesOpsPipelineStages)
      // `type`, `behavior` and `exitKind` are written LITERALLY here and never spread from
      // `data`: a stage minted through this API is always an ordinary open stage.
      .values({
        orgId,
        pipelineId,
        name: data.name,
        status: data.status,
        position,
        type: 'normal',
        behavior: 'none',
        exitKind: null,
        requiresReason: false,
      })
      // The probe above is a time-of-check/time-of-use race: a concurrent writer
      // (an admin double-clicking Save is enough) can land the same name in
      // between, and a plain INSERT would raise 23505 and escape as an HTTP 500
      // instead of the designed 409. Absorbing the conflict here makes
      // sales_ops_pipeline_stages_org_pipeline_name_idx the ACTUAL guard.
      .onConflictDoNothing()
      .returning();
    if (!stage) {
      // Lost the race. Re-probe under a fresh statement snapshot, which now sees
      // the committed winner, and fall back to the name reason.
      return (await findLeadStageClash(tx, orgId, pipelineId, data.name)) ?? ('duplicate' as const);
    }
    await addTransitionsForNewStage(tx, orgId, pipelineId, stage.id);
    return toLeadStageView(stage);
  });
}

/**
 * A new open stage must be reachable: the explicit transition graph says "a pair that is absent is
 * not allowed". The pairs the shared rule (`planPipelineTransitions`) gives the new stage - to and
 * from the previous open stage, to every exit, and from the pausado exit - are inserted; pairs
 * between OTHER stages are never touched, so a graph the org shaped stays as it is.
 */
async function addTransitionsForNewStage(
  tx: Db,
  orgId: string,
  pipelineId: string,
  stageId: string,
): Promise<void> {
  const stages = await listPipelineStages(tx, orgId, pipelineId);
  const plan = stages.map((stage) => ({ ...stage, type: stage.type as TransitionPlanStage['type'], exitKind: stage.exitKind as TransitionPlanStage['exitKind'], status: stage.status as TransitionPlanStage['status'] }));
  const pairs = planPipelineTransitions(plan).filter(([from, to]) => from === stageId || to === stageId);
  if (pairs.length === 0) return;
  await tx
    .insert(salesOpsPipelineTransitions)
    .values(pairs.map(([fromStageId, toStageId]) => ({ orgId, pipelineId, fromStageId, toStageId })))
    .onConflictDoNothing();
}

export function updateLeadStage(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  id: string,
  data: Partial<LeadStageInput>,
): Promise<LeadStageRow | null | 'is_system' | LeadStageDuplicate> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    const [current] = await tx
      .select()
      .from(salesOpsPipelineStages)
      .where(and(eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.id, id)))
      .limit(1)
      .for('update');
    if (!current) return null;
    // Byte-for-byte the funções rule: a system stage (the conversion and the lost etapa) is fully
    // immutable through this endpoint - no rename, no archive, no restore. Its POSITION is still
    // reorderable, which reorderLeadStages does without going through here.
    if (legacyStageIsSystem(current) && (data.name !== undefined || data.status !== undefined)) {
      return 'is_system' as const;
    }

    if (data.name !== undefined) {
      // Fast path for the error message only; the unique index is the real guard.
      const clash = await findLeadStageClash(tx, orgId, current.pipelineId, data.name, id);
      if (clash) return clash;
    }

    // UPDATE has no ON CONFLICT clause, so the same TOCTOU race as
    // createLeadStage is handled by catching the unique violation. The write runs
    // inside a SAVEPOINT (a nested drizzle transaction) so a rejected rename
    // leaves the surrounding transaction usable rather than poisoned.
    let stage: LeadStageRow | null;
    try {
      stage = await tx.transaction(async (nested) => {
        const [row] = await nested
          .update(salesOpsPipelineStages)
          .set({
            ...data,
            ...archivedAtPatch(current.status, data.status ?? current.status),
            updatedAt: new Date(),
          })
          .where(and(eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.id, id)))
          .returning();
        return row ? toLeadStageView(row) : null;
      });
    } catch (error) {
      const violated = mapLeadStageUniqueViolation(error);
      if (violated) return violated;
      throw error;
    }
    // Deliberately NO auditCadastroLifecycle call. The ledger is a closed wire
    // contract whose CadastroEntityTypeSchema is the four audited cadastros, and
    // a fifth entity type would be an entity the history UI cannot render and the
    // nightly purge would eventually hard-delete a stage leads still point at.
    return stage;
  });
}

/**
 * The same rule as sales_ops_areas.archived_at, written locally because
 * `archivedAtPatch` in service.ts is private there. It is set so a future purge
 * has the timestamp it would need; nothing in this feature reads it, and
 * runArchivedCadastroPurge is deliberately NOT extended to lead stages.
 */
function archivedAtPatch(before: string, after: string): { archivedAt?: Date | null } {
  if (before !== 'archived' && after === 'archived') return { archivedAt: new Date() };
  if (before === 'archived' && after !== 'archived') return { archivedAt: null };
  return {};
}

/**
 * Writes a total order over the pipeline's ACTIVE stages, in ONE transaction.
 *
 * - The payload is the COMPLETE ordered set of the pipeline's active stage ids, or it
 *   is rejected. A partial "move stage X to index 3" payload cannot express the
 *   result without the server re-deriving every other position, and two admins
 *   doing that concurrently interleave into an order neither asked for. A total
 *   payload makes the write idempotent and replay safe.
 * - ARCHIVED stages are excluded and keep their stored positions: they are not on
 *   the board, so they have no order to express.
 * - `.for('update')` runs before any write, so two concurrent reorders serialize
 *   at Postgres rather than half-applying each other.
 * - `position` is deliberately NOT unique-indexed. The loop necessarily passes
 *   through states where two rows share an index, and a non-deferrable unique
 *   index would abort the transaction. This endpoint is the only writer of
 *   position after create and it writes a total order, so uniqueness is an
 *   invariant of the writer rather than of the schema.
 * - It writes the stage rows and NOTHING else. No lead row is read or written, so no lead's
 *   stage_changed_at can move, and the transition graph is explicit data: reordering never
 *   regenerates it.
 */
/** Reorders the DEFAULT pipeline's active stages. */
export async function reorderLeadStages(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  stageIds: string[],
): Promise<LeadStageRow[] | 'set_mismatch'> {
  return (await reorderLeadStagesInPipeline(db, orgId, stageIds, undefined)) as LeadStageRow[] | 'set_mismatch';
}

export function reorderLeadStagesInPipeline(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  stageIds: string[],
  pipelineId: string | undefined,
): Promise<LeadStageRow[] | 'set_mismatch' | 'pipeline_not_found'> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    const resolved = await resolvePipeline(tx, orgId, pipelineId);
    if (resolved === null) return (pipelineId === undefined ? 'set_mismatch' : 'pipeline_not_found') as 'set_mismatch';
    const active = await tx
      .select({ id: salesOpsPipelineStages.id })
      .from(salesOpsPipelineStages)
      .where(
        and(
          eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.orgId, orgId),
          eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.pipelineId, resolved),
          eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.status, 'active'),
        ),
      )
      .orderBy(asc(salesOpsPipelineStages.position))
      .for('update');

    // Full-set equality, both directions. The schema already refused duplicates,
    // so equal lengths plus every id known is set equality.
    if (active.length !== stageIds.length) return 'set_mismatch' as const;
    const known = new Set(active.map((row) => row.id));
    if (stageIds.some((id) => !known.has(id))) return 'set_mismatch' as const;

    for (const [index, id] of stageIds.entries()) {
      await tx
        .update(salesOpsPipelineStages)
        .set({ position: index, updatedAt: new Date() })
        .where(and(eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsPipelineStages.id, id)));
    }

    return (await listPipelineStages(tx, orgId, resolved)).map(toLeadStageView);
  });
}

/**
 * The empresa of a write: the top-level `clientId` wins when the body carries it, the Cliente
 * field entry of `fields` applies otherwise. Naming both is ambiguous and refused.
 */
function pickClientId(
  topLevel: string | null | undefined,
  cliente: { clientId: string | null; index: number } | null,
): string | null {
  if (topLevel !== undefined && cliente !== null) throw new LeadInputError('field_invalid', cliente.index);
  if (topLevel !== undefined) return topLevel;
  return cliente?.clientId ?? null;
}

export function updateLead(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  id: string,
  input: LeadPatchInput,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<WriteLeadResult> {
  return applyLeadUpdate(db, orgId, id, input, scope);
}

async function applyLeadUpdate(
  db: Db,
  orgId: string,
  id: string,
  input: LeadPatchInput,
  scope: LeadScope,
): Promise<WriteLeadResult> {
  return withTenant(db, orgId, async (tx) => {
    const gate = await resolveLeadScopePredicate(tx, orgId, scope);
    if (!gate.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gate.reason } as const;

    const [current] = await tx
      .select()
      .from(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      .where(and(...leadIdentityConditions(orgId, id, gate)))
      .limit(1)
      .for('update');
    if (!current) return { ok: false, reason: 'not_found' } as const;
    // A converted card is read-only: its data now lives on the proposta.
    if (current.saleId !== null) return { ok: false, reason: 'already_converted' } as const;

    const claimant = claimantFor(gate, current);
    // Whether this UPDATE writes the seller pair at all: a claim always does,
    // otherwise only a body that names the key.
    const writesSeller = claimant !== null || input.sellerPersonId !== undefined;

    let seller: ResolvedSeller | null = null;
    if (claimant !== null) {
      // On an unassigned lead the caller may leave the vendedor out, clear it or
      // name themselves, and all three mean "mine": the web form of a pool lead
      // carries an empty vendedor. Any other id would hand the lead to a
      // colleague, which only a gestor may do, so it is refused before anything
      // is written. The snapshot is the pessoa's own display name, read here.
      const requested = input.sellerPersonId ?? null;
      if (requested !== null && requested !== claimant) {
        return { ok: false, reason: 'seller_scope' } as const;
      }
      seller = await resolveSellerPersonId(tx, orgId, claimant);
    } else if (input.sellerPersonId !== undefined) {
      const requested = input.sellerPersonId ?? null;
      if (gate.sellerPersonId && requested !== gate.sellerPersonId) {
        return { ok: false, reason: 'seller_scope' } as const;
      }
      seller = requested ? await resolveSellerPersonId(tx, orgId, requested) : null;
    }

    // A write that touches the contact must leave at least one of phone and e-mail.
    if (input.contactPhone !== undefined || input.contactEmail !== undefined) {
      const phone = input.contactPhone !== undefined ? input.contactPhone : current.contactPhone;
      const email = input.contactEmail !== undefined ? input.contactEmail : current.contactEmail;
      if (!phone && !email) throw new LeadInputError('contact_required');
    }

    const dynamic = await resolveFieldWrites(tx, orgId, input.fields ?? []);
    const legacy = await resolveLegacyFieldWrites(tx, orgId, input);
    const clientTouched = input.clientId !== undefined || dynamic.cliente !== null;
    const nextClientId = clientTouched ? pickClientId(input.clientId, dynamic.cliente) : current.clientId;

    let clientNameSnapshot: string | undefined;
    if (clientTouched) {
      clientNameSnapshot = nextClientId
        ? await resolveClientName(tx, orgId, nextClientId)
        : // The column is NOT NULL, so clearing the LINK can never blank the
          // label: the body's text wins, and the stored snapshot is the floor.
          (input.clientName ?? current.clientNameSnapshot);
    } else if (input.clientName !== undefined) {
      // NOT NULL floor: a null clear keeps the stored snapshot, matching the
      // clientId branch above.
      clientNameSnapshot = input.clientName ?? current.clientNameSnapshot;
    }

    await tx
      .update(salesOpsCrmLeads)
      // Built from `input` alone, and `LeadPatchSchema` is `.strict()` and
      // declares no stageId, position, stageChangedAt, saleId or lostReason - so
      // none of those keys can appear here, ever.
      .set({
        ...(input.contactName !== undefined ? { contactName: input.contactName } : {}),
        ...(clientTouched ? { clientId: nextClientId } : {}),
        ...(clientNameSnapshot !== undefined ? { clientNameSnapshot } : {}),
        ...(input.estimatedValueBrl !== undefined
          ? { estimatedValueBrl: input.estimatedValueBrl }
          : {}),
        ...(input.contactPhone !== undefined ? { contactPhone: input.contactPhone } : {}),
        ...(input.contactEmail !== undefined ? { contactEmail: input.contactEmail } : {}),
        ...(writesSeller
          ? {
              sellerPersonId: seller?.id ?? null,
              sellerNameSnapshot: seller?.displayName ?? '',
            }
          : {}),
        updatedAt: new Date(),
      })
      // Live again here, not only in the identity read: belt and braces under READ COMMITTED, the row lock above already re-checked it.
      .where(and(eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.orgId, orgId), eq(salesOpsCrmLeads.id, id), liveLeadCondition()));

    // A key absent from the body leaves its value rows alone; an empty value clears them. The same
    // distinction `updateProduct` draws for the produto's função costs.
    await applyFieldWrites(tx, orgId, id, [...legacy, ...dynamic.writes]);

    return { ok: true, lead: await readLeadView(tx, orgId, id) } as const;
  });
}

  /**

   * walk. Only the import executor passes it. Every other caller is held to the transition graph.
   */
  skipTransitionGraph?: boolean;
};

 * board (`advanceLead` in lead-history-service.ts is the other one, sharing the lock order, the
 * transition rule and the history event).
 */
export async function moveLead(

/** The dynamic renderers of the one LeadForm. The Cliente is never rendered here (LeadForm owns it). */

export type FieldRendererProps = {
  definition: FieldDefinition;
  draft: FieldDraft | undefined;
  onChange: (next: FieldDraft) => void;
  products: SalesOpsProduct[];
  sales: ComboboxOption[];
  today: string;

export type LeadFormFieldsProps = {
  definitions: FieldDefinition[];
  drafts: Record<string, FieldDraft>;
  onChange: (fieldId: string, next: FieldDraft) => void;

const EMPTY_SALES: ComboboxOption[] = [];

function textOf(draft: FieldDraft | undefined): string {
  return typeof draft === 'string' ? draft : '';
}

function itemsOf(draft: FieldDraft | undefined): Array<{ id: string | null; name: string }> {
  return Array.isArray(draft) ? draft : [];
}

function TextRenderer({ definition, draft, onChange }: FieldRendererProps) {
  return (
    <textarea
      className={formTextareaClass}
      id={`lead-field-${definition.id}`}
      maxLength={4000}
      onChange={(event) => onChange(event.target.value)}
      value={textOf(draft)}
    />
  );
}

function NumberRenderer({ definition, draft, onChange }: FieldRendererProps) {
  return (
    <Input
      className={formInputClass}
      id={`lead-field-${definition.id}`}
      inputMode="decimal"
      onChange={(event) => onChange(event.target.value)}
      step="any"
      type="number"
      value={textOf(draft)}
    />
  );
}

function MoneyRenderer({ definition, draft, onChange }: FieldRendererProps) {
  return (
    <Input
      className={formInputClass}
      id={`lead-field-${definition.id}`}
      inputMode="decimal"
      min="0"
      onChange={(event) => onChange(event.target.value)}
      step="0.01"
      type="number"
      value={textOf(draft)}
    />
  );
}

function DateRenderer({ definition, draft, onChange, today }: FieldRendererProps) {
  return (
    <Input
      className={formInputClass}
      id={`lead-field-${definition.id}`}
      max={isBirthDateDefinition(definition) ? today : undefined}
      onChange={(event) => onChange(event.target.value)}
      type="date"
      value={textOf(draft)}
    />
  );
}

function OptionsRenderer({ definition, draft, onChange }: FieldRendererProps) {
  const current = textOf(draft);
  return (
    <Combobox
      aria-label={definition.label}
      className={formSelectClass}
      id={`lead-field-${definition.id}`}
      onChange={(value) => onChange(value)}
      options={[
        { value: '', label: 'Nenhum' },
        ...(definition.options ?? []).map((option) => ({ value: option.key, label: option.label })),
      ]}
      value={current === '' ? null : current}
    />
  );
}

/** A catalog reference: chips, a catalog picker, and a free-text line. */
function CatalogReferenceField({ definition, draft, onChange, products }: FieldRendererProps) {
  const wantsService = definition.referenceTarget === 'servico';
  const items = itemsOf(draft);
  const chosenIds = new Set(items.map((item) => item.id).filter((id): id is string => id !== null));
  const [pickedId, setPickedId] = React.useState('');
  const [free, setFree] = React.useState('');
  const catalogOptions: ComboboxOption[] = products
    .filter((p) => p.status === 'active' && isServiceProduct(p) === wantsService && !chosenIds.has(p.id))
    .map((p) => ({ value: p.id, label: p.name }));
  const productLabel = new Map(products.map((p) => [p.id, p.name]));

  function add(item: { id: string | null; name: string }) {
    const next = definition.multiple ? [...items, item] : [item];
    onChange(next);
  }

  function addCatalog() {
    if (pickedId === '') return;
    const name = productLabel.get(pickedId) ?? '';
    if (name === '') return;
    add({ id: pickedId, name });
    setPickedId('');
  }

  function addFree() {
    const name = free.trim();
    if (name === '') return;
    add({ id: null, name });
    setFree('');
  }

  return (
    <div className="flex flex-col gap-2">
      {items.length > 0 ? (
        <ul className="flex flex-wrap gap-1.5">
          {items.map((item, index) => (
            <li className={chipClass} key={`${item.id ?? 'free'}-${index}`}>
              {item.name}
              <button
                aria-label={`Remover ${item.name}`}
                className="ml-1 text-[#8b8b92]"
                onClick={() => onChange(items.filter((_, i) => i !== index))}
                type="button"
              >
                x
              </button>
            </li>
          ))}
        </ul>
      ) : null}
      <div className="flex items-center gap-2">
        <Combobox
          aria-label={`${definition.label} - catalogo`}
          className={formSelectClass}
          onChange={setPickedId}
          options={catalogOptions}
          placeholder="Selecionar do catálogo"
          searchPlaceholder="Buscar no catálogo..."
          value={pickedId === '' ? null : pickedId}
        />
        <button className={`${secondaryButtonClass} self-stretch`} onClick={addCatalog} type="button">
          Adicionar
        </button>
      </div>
      <div className="flex items-center gap-2">
        <Input
          className={formInputClass}
          id={`lead-field-${definition.id}-free`}
          onChange={(event) => setFree(event.target.value)}
          placeholder="Item livre"
          value={free}
        />
        <button className={`${secondaryButtonClass} shrink-0 self-stretch whitespace-nowrap`} onClick={addFree} type="button">
          + Adicionar item livre
        </button>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

/** A contato reference: one name, stored as a free item. */
function ContatoReferenceField({ definition, draft, onChange }: FieldRendererProps) {
  const name = itemsOf(draft)[0]?.name ?? '';
  return (
    <Input
      className={formInputClass}
      id={`lead-field-${definition.id}`}
      onChange={(event) => {
        const next = event.target.value;
        onChange(next.trim() === '' ? [] : [{ id: null, name: next }]);
      }}
      value={name}
    />
  );
}

/** A venda reference: one proposta picked from the org's list. */
function VendaReferenceField({ definition, draft, onChange, sales }: FieldRendererProps) {
  const current = itemsOf(draft)[0]?.id ?? null;
  if (sales.length === 0) return <p className={mutedStateClass}>Nenhuma venda disponível.</p>;
  return (
    <Combobox
      aria-label={definition.label}
      className={formSelectClass}
      id={`lead-field-${definition.id}`}
      onChange={(value) => {
        const picked = sales.find((option) => option.value === value);
        onChange(picked ? [{ id: picked.value, name: picked.label }] : []);
      }}
      options={sales}
      value={current}
    />
  );
}

const REFERENCE_RENDERERS: Record<Exclude<FieldReferenceTarget, 'cliente'>, (props: FieldRendererProps) => React.JSX.Element> = {
  produto: CatalogReferenceField,
  servico: CatalogReferenceField,
  contato: ContatoReferenceField,
  venda: VendaReferenceField,
};

function ReferenceRenderer(props: FieldRendererProps) {
  const target = props.definition.referenceTarget;
  if (target === null || target === 'cliente') return <p className={mutedStateClass}>Campo indisponível.</p>;
  const Renderer = REFERENCE_RENDERERS[target];
  return <Renderer {...props} />;
}

const FIELD_RENDERERS: Record<FieldKind, (props: FieldRendererProps) => React.JSX.Element> = {
  text: TextRenderer,
  number: NumberRenderer,
  money: MoneyRenderer,
  date: DateRenderer,
  options: OptionsRenderer,
  reference: ReferenceRenderer,
};

export function LeadFormFields({ definitions, drafts, onChange, products, sales = EMPTY_SALES, today }: LeadFormFieldsProps) {
  return (
    <>
      {renderableDefinitions(definitions).map((definition) => {
        const Renderer = FIELD_RENDERERS[definition.kind];
        return (
          <div className="flex flex-col gap-1.5" data-lead-field={definition.key} key={definition.id}>
            <label className={fieldLabelClass} htmlFor={`lead-field-${definition.id}`}>
              {definition.label}
            </label>
            <Renderer
              definition={definition}
              draft={drafts[definition.id]}
              onChange={(next) => onChange(definition.id, next)}
              products={products}
              sales={sales}
              today={today}
            />
          </div>
        );
      })}
    </>
  );
}
